# Dr. Bartender

A full-stack platform for Dr. Bartender's bartending service business. Handles contractor onboarding, client event planning, service proposals with dynamic pricing, Stripe payments, and admin management.

## Tech Stack

| Layer | Technology |
|---|---|
| Backend | Node 26 (pinned via `.node-version`) / Express 4.18 |
| Frontend | React 18 (Create React App) / React Router 6 |
| Database | PostgreSQL (raw SQL via `pg`, no ORM) |
| Auth | JWT + bcryptjs |
| File Storage | Cloudflare R2 (AWS SDK v3) |
| Payments | Stripe (Elements + webhooks) |
| Email | Resend |
| SMS | Twilio |
| AI read (Inbox) | Anthropic via `@anthropic-ai/sdk` + `zod`, reached only through `server/utils/anthropicClient.js` |
| Email feed (Inbox) | The Gmail API, read-only, raw fetch under a service account with domain-wide delegation, reached only through `server/utils/gmailClient.js`; reads the business mailbox, never sends |
| Menu art (words and images) | OpenAI via `openai` (pinned 7.34.0), reached only through `server/utils/openaiClient.js` |
| Menu art (optional image model) | Google Gemini via `@google/genai` (pinned 2.30.0), reached only through `server/utils/geminiClient.js` |
| VA calling (Zul) | Telegram Bot API (raw HTTPS trigger) + Twilio Programmable Voice callback bridge |
| Web Push | `web-push` (VAPID) for staff-portal notifications and the admin phone's per-kind banners |
| WebAuthn | `@simplewebauthn/server` + `@simplewebauthn/browser` for phone-admin passkey unlock (12h device session) |
| Booking / Scheduling | Cal.com (webhook integration; self-hosted target for V2) |
| Rich Text Editor | TipTap (ProseMirror-based WYSIWYG, blog admin) |
| HTML Sanitization | DOMPurify + jsdom (server-side) |
| CSV parsing | `csv-parse` (Check Cherry import pipeline) |
| Styling | Vanilla CSS |
| Error Tracking (server) | `@sentry/node` |
| Error Tracking (client) | `@sentry/react` |

## Prerequisites

- **Node.js** 26 (pinned via `.node-version`; matches what Render runs in prod today). The planned LTS-alignment decision lives in `docs/audit-2026-07-13/migration-plan.yaml` (`def-node-lts`).
- **PostgreSQL** (local instance or remote connection string)
- **Stripe**, **Resend**, **Twilio**, and **Cloudflare R2** accounts (for full functionality — the app runs without them but those features won't work)

## Local Development Setup

With the prerequisites above in place, from the repo root:

```bash
# 1. Install dependencies (server + client)
npm run install:all

# 2. Configure environment
cp .env.example .env
# Fill in DATABASE_URL, JWT_SECRET, and service API keys

# 3. Create the database
createdb dr_bartender

# 4. Seed the admin account
npm run seed

# 5. Start dev servers (Express on :5000, React on :3000)
npm run dev
```

The React dev server proxies `/api` requests to `localhost:5000` automatically.

How the servers and the tests actually run on the dev box (ports, lane worktrees, the shared dev database, the suite runner, the docs generator) is in [`docs/dev.md`](docs/dev.md).

## Environment Variables

Copy `.env.example` to `.env` and fill in values. The tables below are generated from `.env.example` by `npm run docs`: a variable's description is the comment block directly above it in that file, so add or change a variable there and regenerate, and grep the code before trusting any description.

<!-- gen:env -->
Generated by npm run docs; do not edit between the markers.

Each value is the sample .env.example carries, never the default the code uses when the variable is unset.

### Server

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `PORT` | `5000` | PORT: the server port (default: 5000). NODE_ENV: `development` or `production`. |
| `NODE_ENV` | `development` | PORT: the server port (default: 5000). NODE_ENV: `development` or `production`. |

### Database

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `DATABASE_URL` | `postgresql://username:password@localhost:5432/dr_bartender` | Required. PostgreSQL connection string. |

### JWT

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `JWT_SECRET` | `your-super-secret-jwt-key-change-this-in-production-min-32-chars` | Required. Long random string for signing tokens: the token signing key. |
| `UNSUBSCRIBE_SECRET` |  | Optional. Separate signing key for unsubscribe / marketing-link JWTs (365-day lifetime). Falls back to `JWT_SECRET` if unset; set it in prod to isolate a marketing-token compromise from auth. |
| `WEBAUTHN_RP_ID` |  | WebAuthn, for the phone-admin passkey unlock (mobile admin, spec 2026-08-13 section 8; read by `routes/webauthn.js`). Both optional: the code defaults them. WEBAUTHN_RP_ID, the RP ID override, defaults to `admin.drbartender.com` in prod and `localhost` in dev. It is pinned to the admin host on purpose and is never a bare domain: one bundle serves four hosts, and a bare-domain RP ID would let an admin passkey assert from the public/hiring/staff origins. WEBAUTHN_ORIGIN, the expected origin override, defaults to `https://admin.drbartender.com` in prod and to the localhost origins in dev, `http://localhost:3000` + `http://admin.localhost:3000`. |
| `WEBAUTHN_ORIGIN` |  | WebAuthn, for the phone-admin passkey unlock (mobile admin, spec 2026-08-13 section 8; read by `routes/webauthn.js`). Both optional: the code defaults them. WEBAUTHN_RP_ID, the RP ID override, defaults to `admin.drbartender.com` in prod and `localhost` in dev. It is pinned to the admin host on purpose and is never a bare domain: one bundle serves four hosts, and a bare-domain RP ID would let an admin passkey assert from the public/hiring/staff origins. WEBAUTHN_ORIGIN, the expected origin override, defaults to `https://admin.drbartender.com` in prod and to the localhost origins in dev, `http://localhost:3000` + `http://admin.localhost:3000`. |

### Encryption

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `ENCRYPTION_KEY` |  | Required for bank PII. The key for bank-account data at rest: a 64-hex-char (32-byte) AES-256-GCM key encrypting the bank-account PII fields (`server/utils/encryption.js`). Fails closed in prod when unset. Generate with: node -e "console.log(require('crypto').randomBytes(32).toString('hex'))" |

### Client

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `CLIENT_URL` | `http://localhost:3000` | Required. The admin/staff frontend origin, for CORS + admin-facing links (e.g., admin dashboard URLs in admin emails): `http://localhost:3000` in dev, `https://admin.drbartender.com` in prod. |
| `PUBLIC_SITE_URL` | `http://localhost:3000` | Required. The public marketing site origin, used in client-facing token links (proposals, drink plans, invoices, shopping lists): `http://localhost:3000` in dev, `https://drbartender.com` in prod. Use this domain in any URL that gets sent to a customer. Defaults to https://drbartender.com in prod. |
| `STAFF_URL` | `http://localhost:3000` | Optional. Staff portal origin used in hire-confirmation emails + staff calendar/portal links: `http://localhost:3000` in dev, `https://staff.drbartender.com` in prod. Defaults to `https://staff.drbartender.com` (the prod URL) when unset. |
| `REACT_APP_API_URL` | unset | Client build variable, never read by the server: the client-side API base URL, the backend's absolute URL (the Render service). Set it in Vercel and in `client/.env.production`; `client/src/utils/api.js` calls it plus `/api`, and the public pages that fetch outside `api.js` read it too. Unset, the client calls `/api` on its own origin. |

### Admin Seed Account

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `ADMIN_EMAIL` | `<EMAIL>` | Required for seed. ADMIN_EMAIL: the admin account email and admin inbox address. It is the seed account's email and the default `Reply-To` on every client-facing email sent via `sendEmail`; set it to a monitored inbox in prod so client replies do not bounce. Unset, those emails fall through to no `Reply-To` header. ADMIN_PASSWORD: the admin account password, for the seeded admin account (`server/scripts/createAdmin.js`, `server/db/seed.js`). |
| `ADMIN_PASSWORD` | `change-this-password` | Required for seed. ADMIN_EMAIL: the admin account email and admin inbox address. It is the seed account's email and the default `Reply-To` on every client-facing email sent via `sendEmail`; set it to a monitored inbox in prod so client replies do not bounce. Unset, those emails fall through to no `Reply-To` header. ADMIN_PASSWORD: the admin account password, for the seeded admin account (`server/scripts/createAdmin.js`, `server/db/seed.js`). |
| `ADMIN_PHONE` | `+1xxxxxxxxxx` | Optional. E.164 admin hotline: the first ring of the lead-call and consult-call bridges, plus urgent admin SMS (change requests, dead letters, emergency drops). INTERNAL ROUTING ONLY: NEVER print it to a client (client-facing numbers come from `server/utils/companyPhone.js`), and point it at the 312 Google Voice line, never a personal cell; until 2026-10-02 the drink-plan nudge printed it, and it held Dallas's personal cell. Used by the lead-call bridge ring order (`ADMIN_PHONE` → `VA_CELL`), inside-T14 change-request SMS, dispatcher dead-letter alerts, and urgent inbound-SMS alerts. It is also the consult call bridge's first ring: unset there, the chain skips Dallas entirely and opens on Zul's leg with the briefing that never says he missed a call. Unset → those SMS legs are skipped. NOTE it does **not** drive the ≤72h last-minute booking alert, which fans out via `notifyAdminCategory('urgent_booking')` to subscribed admins/managers and never reads this var. |

### SMS (Twilio)

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `TWILIO_ACCOUNT_SID` | `ACxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx` | Required for SMS. The Twilio SMS credentials: TWILIO_ACCOUNT_SID the account SID, TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN the auth token, TWILIO_PHONE_NUMBER the sender number (the 888 line; the two 224 lines are fixed in `server/utils/smsLines.js`). `TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN` is also used to verify the inbound-SMS webhook signature (`POST /api/sms/inbound`) and the message status callback (`POST /api/sms/status`). |
| `TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN` | `your_auth_token_here` | Required for SMS. The Twilio SMS credentials: TWILIO_ACCOUNT_SID the account SID, TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN the auth token, TWILIO_PHONE_NUMBER the sender number (the 888 line; the two 224 lines are fixed in `server/utils/smsLines.js`). `TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN` is also used to verify the inbound-SMS webhook signature (`POST /api/sms/inbound`) and the message status callback (`POST /api/sms/status`). |
| `TWILIO_PHONE_NUMBER` | `+1xxxxxxxxxx` | Required for SMS. The Twilio SMS credentials: TWILIO_ACCOUNT_SID the account SID, TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN the auth token, TWILIO_PHONE_NUMBER the sender number (the 888 line; the two 224 lines are fixed in `server/utils/smsLines.js`). `TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN` is also used to verify the inbound-SMS webhook signature (`POST /api/sms/inbound`) and the message status callback (`POST /api/sms/status`). |
| `INBOX_TEXT_LINES` | `888` | Lines a human reply may text from (the Messages reply and the Inbox reply), as a comma list of `888`, `1922`, `0082`. Default `888`; unknown keys are ignored and the 888 is always on. Add a 224 line only after its round trip (a reply, a STOP and a START) shows up in `sms_messages` with the right `metadata.to` (spec 2026-10-06, decision 19). |

### Payments (Stripe - test mode, optional)

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY_TEST` | `<TOKEN>` | Required for test mode (STRIPE_SECRET_KEY_TEST, STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY_TEST, STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET_TEST). Optional (STRIPE_TEST_MODE_UNTIL). The Stripe test-mode credentials: STRIPE_SECRET_KEY_TEST the test secret key (used while `STRIPE_TEST_MODE_UNTIL` is in the future), STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY_TEST the test publishable key, STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET_TEST the test webhook signing secret. STRIPE_TEST_MODE_UNTIL: an ISO 8601 cutoff date. While it is set and in the future, every Stripe call uses the `*_TEST` credentials below; after the cutoff passes, the next request automatically reverts to the live credentials above, with no redeploy. **The date must be in the FUTURE.** A cutoff already past (or malformed, or empty) reads as "configured for test" and silently means LIVE: real cards, real money, because `isTestMode()` tests `Date.now() < cutoff` and fails open. That is not hypothetical: this file and the README table both used to carry a fixed sample date, it went stale, and a stale sample date was pasted verbatim into a real `.env` on 2026-08-19 and read as "configured for test" while charging live. Use a far-future date so it cannot expire out from under you, e.g. `2099-12-31T23:59:59Z`, and verify `isTestMode()` rather than trusting the file. NOTE the dev box deliberately runs against LIVE Stripe (operator work is done from it: payment links, refunds, customer writes). Leave these unset there unless you specifically want test mode for a session. |
| `STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY_TEST` | `pk_test_xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx` | Required for test mode (STRIPE_SECRET_KEY_TEST, STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY_TEST, STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET_TEST). Optional (STRIPE_TEST_MODE_UNTIL). The Stripe test-mode credentials: STRIPE_SECRET_KEY_TEST the test secret key (used while `STRIPE_TEST_MODE_UNTIL` is in the future), STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY_TEST the test publishable key, STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET_TEST the test webhook signing secret. STRIPE_TEST_MODE_UNTIL: an ISO 8601 cutoff date. Use a far-future date so it cannot expire out from under you, e.g. `2099-12-31T23:59:59Z`, and verify `isTestMode()` rather than trusting the file. NOTE the dev box deliberately runs against LIVE Stripe (operator work is done from it: payment links, refunds, customer writes). Leave these unset there unless you specifically want test mode for a session. |
| `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET_TEST` | `<TOKEN>` | Required for test mode (STRIPE_SECRET_KEY_TEST, STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY_TEST, STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET_TEST). Optional (STRIPE_TEST_MODE_UNTIL). ### Tip QR page

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `PUBLIC_GOOGLE_REVIEW_URL` | `https://g.page/r/PLACEHOLDER/review` | Required for tip pages. The Google review URL for the tip thank-you flow: the public review destination shown after a tip submission. Set the same URL on both the server (for emails / token links) and the client (REACT_APP_*) sides. PUBLIC_GOOGLE_REVIEW_URL is the server-side URL surfaced from the tip thank-you flow; REACT_APP_GOOGLE_REVIEW_URL is the client build-time URL (same value as `PUBLIC_GOOGLE_REVIEW_URL`). |
| `REACT_APP_GOOGLE_REVIEW_URL` | `https://g.page/r/PLACEHOLDER/review` | Required for tip pages. The Google review URL for the tip thank-you flow: the public review destination shown after a tip submission. Set the same URL on both the server (for emails / token links) and the client (REACT_APP_*) sides. PUBLIC_GOOGLE_REVIEW_URL is the server-side URL surfaced from the tip thank-you flow; REACT_APP_GOOGLE_REVIEW_URL is the client build-time URL (same value as `PUBLIC_GOOGLE_REVIEW_URL`). |
| `ADMIN_FEEDBACK_NOTIFICATION_EMAIL` | `contact@drbartender.com` | Required for tip pages. The inbox that receives bartender feedback submissions from the tip thank-you flow. Default: the contact address, the value below. |

### Thumbtack Integration

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `THUMBTACK_WEBHOOK_SECRET` | `your_thumbtack_webhook_secret_here` | Required for Thumbtack. Shared secret for Thumbtack webhook auth. |
| `THUMBTACK_AGENT_SECRET` |  | Required for harvester. Shared secret for the Thumbtack box-agent surface (`/api/admin/thumbtack/*`: email harvesting + auto first-reply): the email-harvester agent (the box-only poller + the admin manual-paste UI, the admin-paste routes) and the auto first-reply. Timing-safe compare; the agent routes **fail closed (401) in every environment when unset**. Generate a long random value; never commit it. BLAST RADIUS (2026-07-21): the secret now also reads customer names (pending-first-replies), can trigger window-bypassed call chains one-shot per pending day lead (first-reply-sent), and can suppress replies (first-reply-failed x3). Leak response: rotate the secret AND flip `TT_AUTOREPLY_ENABLED` + `LEAD_CALL_ENABLED`. |
| `HARVESTER_ENABLED` | `true` | Optional. Kill-switch for the harvester: set to `false` to make `GET /api/admin/thumbtack/pending-harvest` return `[]` (a redeploy-free stop) and to idle the box agent. Defaults on when unset. |
| `MAX_HARVEST_ATTEMPTS` | `3` | Optional. Transient-failure retry cap for the agent before a harvest lead is marked `failed`. Default 3. |
| `HARVEST_COOLDOWN_INTERVAL` | `6 hours` | Optional. Postgres interval before a leased-but-unresolved harvest lead is re-offered by `pending-harvest`. Default `'6 hours'`. |

### Venue search, Inbox and menu art (Google Places, Anthropic, Gmail, OpenAI, Gemini)

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `GOOGLE_PLACES_API_KEY` |  | Required for venue search. Google Places API (New) key for venue-name search. Server-only. When unset, the venue search degrades to a plain text input. |
| `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` |  | Required for the Inbox AI read. Server-only Anthropic key; it enables the Inbox AI read (spec 2026-10-06 section 6) and is read only by `server/utils/anthropicClient.js`. Unset (the dev box, which runs without it) means the read is off: no calls, an unread question stays waiting, replies still close items, and the Inbox feed line says the AI read is off. The read also takes email and Zola lines (spec 2026-10-14 section 6): the subject rides ahead of the words, an attachment is `[attachment]`, never a filename, and an email line keeps 8,000 characters before redaction and 4,000 after (a text 3,000 and 1,500); a Sent mail to several people is read once. The inbound read returns a `solicitation` flag (a vendor pitch, a sponsorship ask, recruiting, a survey): the job stores it in `inbox_reads.solicitation` with `needs_reply` forced true, so a pitch never closes on its own and its row carries the Pitch chip. Personal mail is never read. Set in Render 2026-10-06; replaced 2026-10-14 by a service-account key scoped to the Default Workspace. The key MUST be scoped to one workspace: the server sends no `anthropic-workspace-id` header, so an unscoped key (a multi-workspace personal or service-account key) fails every call with a content-free 400 (the 2026-10-14 switch-on: 59 subjects spent their attempts before a probe with the key named it). Smoke a new key against the real request before it goes into Render (walkthroughs-owed, Inbox). |
| `INBOX_AI_MODEL` | unset | Optional. Model for the AI read: `claude-opus-5-5` (the default, sent at low effort, `output_config.effort: 'low'`, with the server-side refusal fallback, `fallbacks: "default"` under beta `server-side-fallback-2026-07-01`) or `claude-haiku-4-5` (cheaper: about a quarter of the cost; the request then leaves out effort and the fallback, which the claude-api docs do not list for it). Any other value falls back to the default, with one console warning at boot. |
| `INBOX_AI_DAILY_CAP` | unset | Optional. Max AI read ATTEMPTS per rolling 24 hours, counted from `inbox_reads` (default 300; `0`, blank or nonsense fall back to 300, the `LEAD_CALL_DAILY_CAP` precedent). A typical read is about 2 to 3 cents and the ceiling about 10 cents (`max_tokens` 4096 spent on output), so the cap's worst day is about $30. At the cap reads pause, a Sentry warning goes out at most once an hour, and the feed line says paused. The off switches are the key and `RUN_INBOX_READ_SCHEDULER=false`. |
| `RUN_INBOX_READ_SCHEDULER` |  | Optional. Set to `false` to disable the 60-second Inbox AI read scheduler (`inbox_ai_read`, first run 40 s after boot); at `false` the Inbox feed line says the AI read is off. Default on. Honored only when `RUN_SCHEDULERS` is not `false`. Render had it at `false` from 2026-10-09; Dallas set it to `true` on 2026-10-14 and the read is live (the Neon transfer call on the fix list is now measured against real usage). |
| `GMAIL_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY` |  | Required for the Inbox email feed (spec 2026-10-14). Base64 of the service account's JSON key. Server-only, read only by `server/utils/gmailClient.js`; the mailbox it reads is a code constant there. Unset means the Gmail feed is off. Smoke a new key with `scripts/gmail-probe.js` before Render. BLAST RADIUS: under domain-wide delegation the key can read every mailbox in the Workspace, not only the business one. Leak response: delete the key in Google Cloud and remove the delegation row in the Workspace admin console. Keep the downloaded JSON in `~/.secrets/` (chmod 600) and delete it once the value is in Render. |
| `INBOX_OWN_EMAILS` |  | Optional comma list of extra addresses that are "us" and never form an item (the older Gmail address of the business). The mailbox, its aliases and every admin's and manager's address are ours without being listed. With the mailbox and the personal alias, these are also the only addresses the Zola replay gate counts as addressed to us (`gmailRules.isMailboxRecipient`, as written or without a +tag; an admin's or manager's own address, `FROM_EMAIL` and `ADMIN_EMAIL` are not). With the mailbox, the no-reply address `FROM_EMAIL` sends from, `ADMIN_EMAIL` and the own address of every admin and manager but the owner (`PERSONAL_MAILBOX_OWNER_USER_ID`), they are also the business addresses whose presence in To or Cc makes a mail at the personal alias business mail rather than personal (`people.businessEmails`, read by `personalMail.js`). |
| `RUN_GMAIL_SYNC_SCHEDULER` |  | Optional. `false` disables the 60-second Gmail sync. Default on. Honored only when `RUN_SCHEDULERS` is not `false`. |
| `OPENAI_API_KEY` |  | Required for menu art. Enables the menu art generator (spec 2026-10-01). Server-only, read only by `server/utils/openaiClient.js`. Unset means the feature is off: `configured.openai` is false, Generate, re-roll and Update draft answer 503 `MENU_ART_NOT_CONFIGURED` (no Sentry page), and an existing draft can still be viewed, edited and approved. Before it goes into Render, set a monthly budget on the OpenAI project too: that is the provider-side backstop behind the daily cap. Dev and prod share one key, so they share OpenAI's per-minute limit; each database keeps its own cap. The wrapper refuses every call while `OPENAI_CUSTOM_HEADERS` is set (blank included), since the SDK would let it replace the auth headers: leave it unset. |
| `MENU_ART_IMAGE_MODEL` | unset | Optional. OpenAI model ids, stamped on each piece when it is asked for. MENU_ART_IMAGE_MODEL: the OpenAI image model, default `gpt-image-2.5-sunburst`; the job draws with the stamped id, never the env value at run time. MENU_ART_TEXT_MODEL: the structured-output model for the words step, default `gpt-5.4-mini`. |
| `MENU_ART_TEXT_MODEL` | unset | Optional. OpenAI model ids, stamped on each piece when it is asked for. MENU_ART_IMAGE_MODEL: the OpenAI image model, default `gpt-image-2.5-sunburst`; the job draws with the stamped id, never the env value at run time. MENU_ART_TEXT_MODEL: the structured-output model for the words step, default `gpt-5.4-mini`. |
| `MENU_ART_BACKGROUND_SIZE` | unset | Optional. OpenAI background size, default `2560x3200`; `1664x2080` is the non-experimental fallback. |
| `MENU_ART_DAILY_IMAGE_CAP` | unset | Optional. Rolling 24-hour ceilings counted from `menu_art_calls`. MENU_ART_DAILY_IMAGE_CAP: image reservations across all events and BOTH providers, counted from `menu_art_calls` rows of kind `image` not `released` (default 60). At the cap Generate, re-roll and Update draft answer 429 `MENU_ART_CAP_REACHED` and nothing is called. About $15 on a worst day on OpenAI, about $14.40 on Gemini. MENU_ART_DAILY_TEXT_CAP: words calls, Generate and Update draft (default 100); at the cap 429 `MENU_ART_TEXT_CAP_REACHED`. Text rows never count against the image cap. These two and the two below (`MENU_ART_IMAGE_TIMEOUT_MS`, `MENU_ART_STALE_MS`) parse through `server/utils/envInt.js`: `0`, blank or nonsense falls back to the default with one warning, so a typo can never uncap a provider. |
| `MENU_ART_DAILY_TEXT_CAP` | unset | Optional. Rolling 24-hour ceilings counted from `menu_art_calls`. MENU_ART_DAILY_IMAGE_CAP: image reservations across all events and BOTH providers, counted from `menu_art_calls` rows of kind `image` not `released` (default 60). At the cap Generate, re-roll and Update draft answer 429 `MENU_ART_CAP_REACHED` and nothing is called. About $15 on a worst day on OpenAI, about $14.40 on Gemini. MENU_ART_DAILY_TEXT_CAP: words calls, Generate and Update draft (default 100); at the cap 429 `MENU_ART_TEXT_CAP_REACHED`. Text rows never count against the image cap. These two and the two below (`MENU_ART_IMAGE_TIMEOUT_MS`, `MENU_ART_STALE_MS`) parse through `server/utils/envInt.js`: `0`, blank or nonsense falls back to the default with one warning, so a typo can never uncap a provider. |
| `MENU_ART_IMAGE_TIMEOUT_MS` | unset | Optional. MENU_ART_IMAGE_TIMEOUT_MS: the per image call timeout on both providers (default 180000). Text calls use a fixed 60000. MENU_ART_STALE_MS: the heartbeat age at which a pending or working piece, or the words step, reads as failed ("interrupted") and its re-roll is allowed (default 300000, above one image timeout plus one retry wait). Covers a server restart, which skips the job's end-of-run sweep. These four menu art numbers (the two daily caps above and these two) must be whole positive numbers: `0`, blank or nonsense falls back to the default with one warning, so a typo can never uncap a provider. |
| `MENU_ART_STALE_MS` | unset | Optional. MENU_ART_IMAGE_TIMEOUT_MS: the per image call timeout on both providers (default 180000). Text calls use a fixed 60000. MENU_ART_STALE_MS: the heartbeat age at which a pending or working piece, or the words step, reads as failed ("interrupted") and its re-roll is allowed (default 300000, above one image timeout plus one retry wait). Covers a server restart, which skips the job's end-of-run sweep. These four menu art numbers (the two daily caps above and these two) must be whole positive numbers: `0`, blank or nonsense falls back to the default with one warning, so a typo can never uncap a provider. |
| `GEMINI_API_KEY` |  | Optional. Enables the Gemini option of the menu art image-model toggle. Server-only, read only by `server/utils/geminiClient.js`. Unset means `configured.gemini` is false, the toggle's Gemini option is disabled, a `gemini` request answers 503 `MENU_ART_GEMINI_NOT_CONFIGURED`, and OpenAI is unaffected. In Render it must come from a billing-enabled AI Studio project (paid tier, so prompts and images are not used for training) with a hard ceiling: prepay without auto-refill, or a project spend cap; preferably a separate project from the second-opinion reviewer's, so menu art and code review do not share a spend limit. On the dev box the live smoke and the walks pass the reviewer's paid key from `~/.gemini/.env` into the lane server's process environment only, bounded by the dev database's own cap; it never goes into the root `.env`. BLAST RADIUS: the key can draw images billed to that project. Leak response: delete the key in AI Studio and unset it in Render; OpenAI keeps working. The wrapper refuses every call while `GOOGLE_GENAI_DEBUG` (it would log the key) or `GOOGLE_GENAI_USER_PROJECT` (it would move the billing) is set: leave both unset. |
| `MENU_ART_GEMINI_IMAGE_MODEL` | unset | Optional. Gemini image model for backgrounds and drinks, default `gemini-3-pro-image`. Only that id is supported (it takes style references); other ids are untested. |

### Render

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `RENDER_EXTERNAL_URL` | unset | Auto-set by Render in production; do not set it locally. The service's own public URL, which the backend origin `API_URL` falls back to (`server/utils/urls.js`). |
| `RENDER_GIT_COMMIT` | unset | Auto-set by Render in production; do not set it locally. The deployed commit's sha, which `/api/health` returns as `release` and `Sentry.init` sends as the release (`server/index.js`). |
| `API_URL` | unset | Optional. Backend origin for server-rendered email links (unsubscribe, etc). Defaults to `RENDER_EXTERNAL_URL` in prod, `http://localhost:5000` in dev; set it explicitly if the backend ever moves behind a CDN or custom domain. |

### Sentry

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `SENTRY_DSN_SERVER` |  | Required for error tracking. Leave unset in dev to disable error reporting. SENTRY_DSN_SERVER: the server-side Sentry DSN. REACT_APP_SENTRY_DSN_CLIENT: the client-side Sentry DSN. Each is optional in dev and required in prod. |
| `REACT_APP_SENTRY_DSN_CLIENT` |  | Required for error tracking. Leave unset in dev to disable error reporting. SENTRY_DSN_SERVER: the server-side Sentry DSN. REACT_APP_SENTRY_DSN_CLIENT: the client-side Sentry DSN. Each is optional in dev and required in prod. |

### Scheduler and outbound-send gates

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `RUN_SCHEDULERS` |  | Schedulers fire only when `NODE_ENV=production` (Render sets this by default). In any other environment they default to OFF, so a local dev server never burns Resend/Twilio allotments by iterating the shared Neon DB. RUN_SCHEDULERS=true force-on locally (testing a handler against a scratch row) RUN_SCHEDULERS=false force-off on a secondary prod instance to prevent duplicate runs |
| `SEND_NOTIFICATIONS` |  | Real outbound notifications, email (Resend), SMS (Twilio) and Web Push, fire only when `NODE_ENV=production` by default (the same philosophy as `RUN_SCHEDULERS`), so a local dev server never burns provider allotments (Resend/Twilio) against the shared Neon DB. Web Push rides the same gate since 2026-10-14. SEND_NOTIFICATIONS=true force real sends locally (e.g. a real send to a scratch row) SEND_NOTIFICATIONS=false force off everywhere (e.g. a secondary prod instance) When gated off, `sendEmail`/`sendSMS` take their existing log-and-skip path, and `sendPush` (`pushSender.js`) logs the push service's host and returns `{ ok: false, error: 'notifications_off' }`, so a dev server on the shared DB never buzzes a real phone. |

### Cal.com (consult booking)

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `CAL_WEBHOOK_SECRET` |  | Required for Cal.com. HMAC-SHA256 signing secret for the Cal.com webhook; required in prod. Fails closed: the webhook returns 503 if unset. See docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-26-cal-com-integration-design.md. |
| `CAL_BOOKING_URL` |  | Required for Cal.com. The public Cal.com booking page URL. Hosted: https://cal.com/<username>/<event-type>. Self-hosted: https://book.drbartender.com/<event-type>. Surfaced in 3 client comms touches (drink-plan nudge email + SMS, six-months-out marketing). Optional in dev; templates omit the consult line when unset. |

### Sweeps and monitors (per-scheduler switches)

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `RUN_WEBHOOK_EVENTS_PRUNE_SCHEDULER` |  | Optional. Set to `false` to disable the hourly `webhook_events` 30-day prune scheduler. Default on. Honored only when `RUN_SCHEDULERS` is not `false`. |
| `RUN_PENDING_EMAIL_CLEANUP_SCHEDULER` |  | Optional. Set to `false` to disable the daily `pending_email_changes` 7-day purge, the cleanup scheduler (spec §6.10). Default on. Honored only when `RUN_SCHEDULERS` is not `false`. |
| `RUN_STRIPE_PAYOUT_SWEEP_SCHEDULER` |  | Optional. Set to `false` to disable the daily Stripe payout mirror sweep (webhook-miss heal, pending bucket, re-match). Default on (prod only, like all schedulers). Honored only when `RUN_SCHEDULERS` is not `false`. |
| `RUN_REFUND_PENDING_SWEEP_SCHEDULER` |  | Optional. Set to `false` to disable the 15-minute stale-pending-refund sweep, which heals aged `pending` `proposal_refunds` rows: it reconciles rows stuck `pending` >30 min against Stripe (`stripe.refunds.list`), adopting the real, matching refund, or marks them failed after that 30-min age gate. Default on. Honored only when `RUN_SCHEDULERS` is not `false`. |
| `RUN_STALE_PROPOSAL_SWEEP_SCHEDULER` |  | **Opt-in, not opt-out: the only scheduler flag that works this way**, unlike every other RUN_* flag above. Must be exactly `true` to run the hourly stale-proposal sweep, which archives past-dated proposals that were never booked (`archive_reason = 'event_passed'`), voids their unpaid invoices, reaps shifts, and deletes their pending scheduled messages. Unset keeps it dark even when `RUN_SCHEDULERS=true`, because its first run voids ~105 invoices and makes ~220 LIVE Stripe calls, and this box talks to live Stripe by design. |
| `STALE_PROPOSAL_SWEEP_DRY_RUN` |  | Set to `true` to make the stale-proposal sweep above log its candidate list and the past-dated `accepted` proposals it would leave for an admin, while writing nothing, sending nothing, and calling no Stripe. Use it before the first real run. |
| `RUN_SERVICE_EXTENSION_SWEEP_SCHEDULER` |  | Optional. Set to `false` to disable the 60-second service-extension expiry sweep: it expires a pending request past its grace window (single-winner claim against the payment webhook), voids its invoice, and sends the staff decline that carries the insurance warning. The same tick heals crashed settles (settled-but-unfinalized rows, `finalized_at` NULL). Default on. Honored only when `RUN_SCHEDULERS` is not `false`. |
| `RUN_SHIFT_CLOSURE_SWEEP_SCHEDULER` |  | Optional. Set to `false` to disable the hourly shift-closure sweep (`shiftClosureSweep.js`), which closes a shift to `completed` once the SHIFT's own end instant has passed AND its proposal is `completed`. It NEVER writes `cancelled`: that value is an EVENT-cancelled signal read by the Events dashboard chip and by the iCal feed (STATUS:CANCELLED), so writing it on a delivered event strikes that event off the owner's subscribed calendar. Default on. Honored only when `RUN_SCHEDULERS` is not `false`. |
| `RUN_BALANCE_INVOICE_MONITOR` |  | Optional. Set to `false` to disable the hourly balance-invoice monitor (`balanceInvoiceMonitor.js`): it alerts when a client is billed MORE than they owe, or owes a balance with no payable invoice. ALERT ONLY: it never creates/edits/voids an invoice. Default on. Honored only when `RUN_SCHEDULERS` is not `false`. |
| `RUN_PRESENCE_SCHEDULER` |  | Optional. Set to `false` to disable the presence tracker sweep (every 15 min: the stale-desk nudge via Telegram/SMS + the auto-flip to away on silence). Default on. Honored only when `RUN_SCHEDULERS` is not `false`. |

### Web Push (VAPID) - staff portal push notifications (spec §6.17)

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` |  | Required for push. The Web Push (VAPID) keypair for staff-portal push notifications (spec §6.17). Generate it once with `npx web-push generate-vapid-keys`. VAPID identifies this app server to the browsers' push services. VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY: the public key, shared with the client: the same value is exposed to the client as `REACT_APP_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`. Since 2026-10-14 the admin phone's push uses the same pair: the admin phone reads the public key from `GET /api/me/notification-preferences` (`push.public_key`, served only when the private key is also set), so the admin push needs nothing in Vercel. VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY: the private key, server-only: never commit it, never expose it to the client. When it is unset the push sender fails closed (returns `vapid_unset`) and the server still boots normally; SMS + email keep covering every notification. Push is purely additive. Sends are also gated by `SEND_NOTIFICATIONS` (above). |
| `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` |  | Required for push. The Web Push (VAPID) keypair for staff-portal push notifications (spec §6.17). Generate it once with `npx web-push generate-vapid-keys`. VAPID identifies this app server to the browsers' push services. VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY: the public key, shared with the client: the same value is exposed to the client as `REACT_APP_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`. Since 2026-10-14 the admin phone's push uses the same pair: the admin phone reads the public key from `GET /api/me/notification-preferences` (`push.public_key`, served only when the private key is also set), so the admin push needs nothing in Vercel. VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY: the private key, server-only: never commit it, never expose it to the client. When it is unset the push sender fails closed (returns `vapid_unset`) and the server still boots normally; SMS + email keep covering every notification. Push is purely additive. Sends are also gated by `SEND_NOTIFICATIONS` (above). |
| `REACT_APP_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` |  | Required for staff push. The client-side copy of `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` (the identical value), exposed to the client build (`client/.env*`) and used by the staff portal to subscribe the browser to push. Set it on the client side (Vercel). Staff portal only; the admin phone never reads it. |
| `VAPID_CONTACT_EMAIL` | `contact@drbartender.com` | Required for push. The code treats it as optional and defaults it, as the next line says. Contact email embedded in the VAPID JWT (mailto:). Defaults to contact@drbartender.com. |

### Zul VA Calling (Telegram-triggered Twilio callback bridge)

Our PH-based VA (Zul) places/receives US calls via a Twilio callback bridge: the OUTBOUND trigger is a Telegram bot message; voice rides her CELL (Twilio calls her, she answers, Twilio bridges the second leg to the target with the 224 as caller ID). This endpoint dials billed international calls on an auto-refill account from external input, so it is a toll-fraud target; every guard (secret path + secret_token header + user_id allowlist + US-only NANP validation + confirm-before-dial + spend caps) is load-bearing. See docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-01-zul-va-calling-design.md and docs/va-calling-runbook.md.

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` |  | Required for VA calling. Telegram Bot API token from @BotFather, for the Zul VA-calling trigger. When unset, the Telegram helpers `sendTelegramMessage`/`setTelegramWebhook` no-op (log + skip) and the outbound trigger, so all outbound calling, is dead. |
| `TELEGRAM_WEBHOOK_SECRET` |  | Required for VA calling. Doubles as the secret, unguessable URL path segment (`/api/telegram/<secret>`) AND the value compared constant-time against the `X-Telegram-Bot-Api-Secret-Token` header on every update. Set the SAME value at `setWebhook`. Never commit it. Unset → `verifyTelegramSecret` returns false (all updates 403). Generate: node -e "console.log(require('crypto').randomBytes(24).toString('hex'))" |
| `TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_USER_ID` |  | Required (bootstrap). Numeric Telegram user id of Zul: the ONLY sender allowed to trigger a call. BOOTSTRAP: leave UNSET on the first deploy. **When UNSET the webhook runs in bootstrap mode**: it replies to any sender with their own user id and dials nothing. Read Zul's id from that reply, set it here, redeploy. Once set, every other sender is a silent no-op. |
| `VOICE_CALLER_ID` | `+12242220082` | Required for VA calling. The 224 US voice line ending 0082, in strict E.164 (the value on the line below). Caller ID on Zul's outbound calls AND her inbound line, the number clients dial inbound to reach her; since Phase 1a the number ADVERTISED to clients for calls is the 1922 (`COMPANY_PHONE`). Voice+SMS capable; no SMS-to-clients from it. |
| `VA_CELL` |  | Required for VA calling. Zul's cell in strict E.164 (`+63…`), the bridge target Twilio calls. **NEVER run it through `normalizePhone`** (US-centric; it would mangle a PH number). Stored only here: never on a DB record, never committed to the repo. |

### 224-inbound voicemail (spec 2026-07-22)

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `VOICEMAIL_ENABLED` | `false` | 224-inbound voicemail master switch, **DEFAULT OFF**: only the literal string `'true'` enables it. Off means a missed inbound call hangs up exactly as it did before the feature: no Telegram ping, no greeting, no recording. Ships dark; flip it in Render after the live call test. |
| `VM_MAX_LENGTH_SEC` | `120` | Max voicemail recording length in seconds (default 120, parsed and clamped to 30..300). Per-call recording spend cap. |
| `VM_DAILY_CAP` | `50` | Max voicemail-path calls per rolling 24h (default 50), counted from `voicemail_delivery`. The inbound analog of `VA_CALL_DAILY_CAP`: a missed call used to cost nothing after ring timeout, and now costs greeting + up to `VM_MAX_LENGTH_SEC` of billed recording. On trip the missed handler hangs up and sends no ping. |
| `VM_GREETING_URL` | unset | Optional. Source of Zul's DAY voicemail greeting on the 224 line. Unset (default) → her BUNDLED 2026-08-19 re-recording, `server/assets/zul-greeting-day.mp3`, served at `/api/voice/audio/zul-greeting-day.mp3`. The literal `say` → the synthetic Zul text in the `Polly.Joanna-Neural` voice (a known-good kill switch if a recording regresses), which mirrors the recording word for word and therefore also offers press 1. A full https URL → `<Play>` it without a redeploy. Her bundled default and the synthetic text BOTH say the press-1 offer, which is what stops the synthetic prompt being appended after it. An override URL is assumed NOT to say it, so the offer IS appended after one. The older 2026-07-24 take (`voicemail-greeting.mp3`) is still served at the public `GET /api/voice/greeting.mp3` for anything referencing that URL directly, but it is no longer any slot's default. |

### Phone system Phase 1a (spec 2026-07-26)

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `VM_PRIMARY_DIAL_TARGET` | `+1xxxxxxxxxx` | Required*. (*Required once the 1922's voice webhook points at us.) What the primary line (+12242221922) dials to reach Dallas. Strict E.164. Normally the 312 Google Voice (GV) number, which rings his phone and keeps his personal cell private. Unset means the primary line apologizes and hangs up rather than emitting an empty `<Dial>`, and pages Sentry (throttled): primary calls are apologized away. |
| `VM_PRIMARY_RING_SEC` | `18` | Ring seconds on the primary line before Twilio calls it a miss (default 18, clamped 5..30). Deliberately SHORTER than a typical carrier/Google Voice (GV) voicemail pickup (~25s), so their voicemail can never intercept ours: if their voicemail answers first, Twilio reports completed and the caller lands in a dumb voicemail we cannot route. |
| `VM_TEXT_DESTINATION` | `+1xxxxxxxxxx` | Required* (the same footnote as VM_PRIMARY_DIAL_TARGET: once the 1922's voice webhook points at us). Where a voicemail on the primary line is texted (the 312), strict E.164. Sent from `TWILIO_PHONE_NUMBER` (the 888 until Phase 2) with `skipLog`, so this internal ops alert never lands in a client's message ledger. Unset or malformed SILENTLY DISABLES primary-line delivery: rows stay retryable, but nothing sends and only a startup warning + per-call log says why. The consult call bridge reuses it as its text destination, for its missed-consult and client-did-not-answer texts, falling back to `ADMIN_PHONE`; when neither is strict E.164, or the text fails, the missed chain and the client-did-not-answer report each send the admin email instead, so the consult is never invisible. |
| `VM_LISTEN_LINK_ENABLED` | `true` | Listen-link kill switch (spec 2026-08-10), **default ON**: only the literal `'false'` disables it. TWO-SIDED: off → `GET /api/voice/vm/:token` closes (it 404s) AND the primary alert SMS omits the link line; one `listenLinkEnabled()` in `utils/voicemail.js` feeds both, so they cannot drift and flipping it never strands a dead URL in an alert. The route serves a client's recorded voice to whoever holds the token, so it gets the same redeploy-free off switch as the other PII surfaces. The token IS the auth, and the link is only ever sent to `VM_TEXT_DESTINATION` (the operator, who can already hear every voicemail in the Twilio console): that, NOT the proposal/invoice precedent, is what makes a bare UUID acceptable on third-party audio. Links expire at 30 days, enforced in the route's own SQL. To revoke ONE link instead of all of them: UPDATE voicemail_delivery SET listen_token = gen_random_uuid() WHERE call_sid = $1; |
| `VM_GREETING_URL_PRIMARY` | unset | Dallas's DAY greeting, same contract as `VM_GREETING_URL` but for the primary line. Unset → his BUNDLED recording (`server/assets/primary-greeting-day.mp3`, recorded 2026-08-19); `say` → the synthetic Dallas text; a full https URL → `<Play>` it. Never falls back to Zul's recording. The bundled default and the synthetic text BOTH say the press-1 offer. An override URL is assumed NOT to, so the offer is appended after it. |
| `VM_ESCALATION_ENABLED` | `false` | Press-1 escalation master switch, **default OFF** (only `'true'` enables). What it guarantees, precisely: NO escalation is ever claimed and NO billed leg is ever dialed. `voiceEscalate.js` tests this switch itself, so that holds no matter what the caller presses. What it does NOT do, since 2026-08-19, is remove the OFFER from the caller experience: both lines' day greetings say "press one" inside the recording, and no env var can edit an mp3. Until 2026-08-20 flipping this off also dropped the `<Gather>`, which made the switch a trap: callers were invited to press a key nobody was listening for, pressed 1 into a document that was not listening, and dropped to the beep. The `<Gather>` now follows the GREETING rather than this switch (fix 2026-08-20), so off still emits the `<Gather>` in the DAY, and with escalation off a pressed digit plays the `escalate_failed` recording ("sorry, nobody's available right now") and records, exactly as the quiet window and the daily cap already do. Night emits no `<Gather>` either way. The old claim (off meaning no `<Gather>` on either line, the zul document "byte-identical to pre-feature production", golden-pinned) died with the 2026-08-19 re-recording; the golden pins now assert the CURRENT documents. TWO CORRECTIONS LIVE HERE, and the second one corrects the first attempt at the first one. Read both before prescribing anything about this switch. (a) The 2026-08-19 note prescribed VM_ESCALATION_ENABLED=false + VM_NIGHT_WINDOW=none + VM_GREETING_URL_PRIMARY=say as "the real three-variable revert". It does not work: 'say' resolves to the SYNTHETIC text, which mirrors the recording word for word and so also says "press one". It only changes whose voice makes the offer. (b) The 2026-08-20 replacement for (a) was ALSO WRONG, found by the review fleet the same evening. It said to record a no-offer greeting, point VM_GREETING_URL_PRIMARY / VM_GREETING_URL at it, and flip that slot's defaultSaysOffer to false. Setting the env var DEFEATS the flag: both dayGreetingOffersPress1 and needsAppendedOffer short-circuit on a playable URL and return before defaultSaysOffer is ever read, because an override recording's script is unknown and the safe assumption is that it offers. So the <Gather> stays; and with escalation ON the synthetic offer is APPENDED to your new no-offer recording, re-adding in Polly's voice the exact sentence you recorded it to remove. THE RECIPE THAT ACTUALLY WORKS, and it is a code change, not a config one: replace the slot's BUNDLED file in voicemailTwiml.js SLOTS with a recording that does not say the offer, flip that slot's defaultSaysOffer to false, and leave the env var UNSET so the table is what answers. voice.js then stops emitting the <Gather> for that line by day with no further edit, and a route test pins exactly that. There is still no env-only way to remove the offer. |
| `VM_ESCALATION_DAILY_CAP` | `25` | Max escalation legs per rolling 24h (default 25), enforced as a HARD bound: the cap-check and the claim run in one serialized transaction, serialized on an advisory lock. Toll-fraud bound: a press-1 is a billed outbound leg, and on the primary line it is INTERNATIONAL. |
| `VM_ESCALATION_QUIET_ZUL` | `22:00-08:00` | Quiet hours for the person being escalated TO, the escalation TARGET (ZUL for Zul, PRIMARY for Dallas on the primary line), local to them, in STRICT `HH:MM-HH:MM` (leading zeros required; `24:00` and en dashes are invalid and are treated, with a warning, as no window). Inside the window the escalation is skipped and the caller goes straight to voicemail. Windows may wrap midnight. |
| `VM_ESCALATION_QUIET_PRIMARY` | `22:00-08:00` | Quiet hours for the person being escalated TO, the escalation TARGET (ZUL for Zul, PRIMARY for Dallas on the primary line), local to them, in STRICT `HH:MM-HH:MM` (leading zeros required; `24:00` and en dashes are invalid and are treated, with a warning, as no window). Inside the window the escalation is skipped and the caller goes straight to voicemail. Windows may wrap midnight. |
| `VM_ESCALATION_TZ_ZUL` | unset | Optional IANA zone overrides for those quiet windows (defaults Asia/Manila for Zul, America/Chicago for Dallas). A BAD zone name silently disables the window (fail-open on a live call path; a per-call warning is the only signal). |
| `VM_ESCALATION_TZ_PRIMARY` | unset | Optional IANA zone overrides for those quiet windows (defaults Asia/Manila for Zul, America/Chicago for Dallas). A BAD zone name silently disables the window (fail-open on a live call path; a per-call warning is the only signal). |
| `VM_ESCALATION_PROMPT` | unset | Manual override suppressing the APPENDED press-1 offer everywhere. Since 2026-08-19 the day greetings say the offer themselves, so whether to append is decided from the slot table: `needsAppendedOffer()` trusts only the KNOWN defaults, and BOTH lines now default to 2026-08-19 recordings that say it, so nothing is appended by default and this switch should not be needed. An override URL has UNKNOWN content and DOES get the offer appended, on either line (a doubled offer is an obvious stutter; a missing one is invisible). Set this to suppress that after wiring an override recording that already announces the option. |
| `VM_NIGHT_WINDOW` | unset | Caller communication (spec 2026-08-19). The night window for the CALLER-facing experience: inside it the greeting drops the press-1 offer and promises the morning instead. Strict `HH:MM-HH:MM`, may wrap midnight. Default `21:00-09:00`. The literal `none` disables night entirely, so night mode can be turned off without an invalid value that warns per call. NOT the same thing as `VM_ESCALATION_QUIET_*`, which protect the person being DIALED, in THEIR timezone, and suppress only the dial. This one is about the caller and changes the greeting itself. An unparseable value fails OPEN to day, so the failure mode is a phone that rings, never a caller silently denied the offer of a human. |
| `VM_NIGHT_TZ` | unset | IANA zone for `VM_NIGHT_WINDOW` (the window above), default `America/Chicago`. A bad zone name fails open to day. |
| `VM_NIGHT_GREETING_URL_PRIMARY` | unset | The four message slots per line. Since 2026-08-19 BOTH lines default to their owner's bundled recordings for all four slots, so none of these has to be set; the synthetic texts are the kill switch, not the normal path. Same contract as `VM_GREETING_URL` throughout: unset uses the default, the literal 'say' forces synthetic (the kill switch for a bad recording), a full https URL is <Play>ed. This pair is the night greeting per line; the primary defaults to the bundled `primary-greeting-night.mp3`. WARNING, and nothing in the code can enforce it: a NIGHT greeting must never mention press 1. The day path detects an unknown override and listens anyway (dayGreetingOffersPress1 assumes a recording it cannot read DOES offer), but night is hard-coded to emit no <Gather> at all, switch on or off, and the night slots carry no defaultSaysOffer flag for anything to check. The asymmetry is deliberate -- a night press-1 rings a sleeping phone -- but it means a night recording that offers a human recreates exactly the trap the day path closed on 2026-08-20, silently. Listen to any night recording before pointing these here. |
| `VM_NIGHT_GREETING_URL` | unset | The four message slots per line. Since 2026-08-19 BOTH lines default to their owner's bundled recordings for all four slots, so none of these has to be set; the synthetic texts are the kill switch, not the normal path. Same contract as `VM_GREETING_URL` throughout: unset uses the default, the literal 'say' forces synthetic (the kill switch for a bad recording), a full https URL is <Play>ed. This pair is the night greeting per line; the primary defaults to the bundled `primary-greeting-night.mp3`. WARNING, and nothing in the code can enforce it: a NIGHT greeting must never mention press 1. The day path detects an unknown override and listens anyway (dayGreetingOffersPress1 assumes a recording it cannot read DOES offer), but night is hard-coded to emit no <Gather> at all, switch on or off, and the night slots carry no defaultSaysOffer flag for anything to check. The asymmetry is deliberate -- a night press-1 rings a sleeping phone -- but it means a night recording that offers a human recreates exactly the trap the day path closed on 2026-08-20, silently. Listen to any night recording before pointing these here. |
| `VM_ESCALATE_ACK_URL_PRIMARY` | unset | The press-1 acknowledgment per line, played the instant a caller presses 1, BEFORE any ringing. The primary defaults to the bundled `primary-escalate-ack.mp3`. |
| `VM_ESCALATE_ACK_URL` | unset | The press-1 acknowledgment per line, played the instant a caller presses 1, BEFORE any ringing. The primary defaults to the bundled `primary-escalate-ack.mp3`. |
| `VM_ESCALATE_FAILED_URL_PRIMARY` | unset | The escalation-failed message per line, played whenever escalation returns the caller to voicemail (seven of the eight branches; the rate-limiter handler carries the same message inline). The primary defaults to the bundled `primary-escalate-failed.mp3`. |
| `VM_ESCALATE_FAILED_URL` | unset | The escalation-failed message per line, played whenever escalation returns the caller to voicemail (seven of the eight branches; the rate-limiter handler carries the same message inline). The primary defaults to the bundled `primary-escalate-failed.mp3`. |

### Call caps and call bridges (VA calling, lead calls, consult calls, Thumbtack first-reply)

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `VA_INBOUND_PER_MIN_CAP` | `30` | Global inbound-call flood cap per minute (default 30 per line) on `POST /api/voice/inbound` AND `POST /api/voice/inbound/primary`, each line in its own bucket so a storm on one number cannot silence the other; on trip it returns busy TwiML and never dials. Runs BEHIND the signature gate so unsigned floods cannot consume it. The voicemail and escalation webhooks have their own per-`CallSid` limiters that do not read this var. |
| `RUN_VA_CALLING_SCHEDULER` |  | Optional. Set to `false` to disable the VA-calling maintenance scheduler: the hourly prune of expired `pending_call` + retention-aged `call_audit`/`telegram_update`/`voicemail_delivery` rows, the undelivered-voicemail redelivery sweep, the lead-call stale reaper, and the ~6h Telegram webhook-heartbeat check. The sweep is the ONLY retry for a voicemail stranded between claim and upload, so turning this off while `VOICEMAIL_ENABLED` is on leaves that feature with no recovery path. It no longer touches consult rows: the consult-call reaper moved to the consult sweep (spec 2026-09-30), see `RUN_CONSULT_CALL_SWEEP_SCHEDULER`. Default on. Honored only when `RUN_SCHEDULERS` is not `false` (the global flag wins). (declared twice, lines 460 and 727; dotenv keeps the last) |
| `VA_CALL_DAILY_CAP` | `40` | Optional spend caps (toll-fraud controls). Defaults shown. When the daily or the per-minute cap trips, the bot tells Zul and no call is placed. VA_CALL_DAILY_CAP max calls placed per rolling 24h (default 40, DB-backed by counting `call_audit`) VA_CALL_PER_MIN_CAP max triggers accepted per minute (default 5) VA_CALL_TIME_LIMIT_SEC per-call hard `timeLimit` on BOTH call legs (default 1800 = 30 min); both the lead-call and consult-call bridges reuse the same value, on their agent legs and on their press-1 bridges PENDING_CALL_TTL_SEC how long a confirm-before-dial pending record lives: its TTL in seconds (default 120) |
| `VA_CALL_PER_MIN_CAP` | `5` | Optional spend caps (toll-fraud controls). Defaults shown. When the daily or the per-minute cap trips, the bot tells Zul and no call is placed. VA_CALL_DAILY_CAP max calls placed per rolling 24h (default 40, DB-backed by counting `call_audit`) VA_CALL_PER_MIN_CAP max triggers accepted per minute (default 5) VA_CALL_TIME_LIMIT_SEC per-call hard `timeLimit` on BOTH call legs (default 1800 = 30 min); both the lead-call and consult-call bridges reuse the same value, on their agent legs and on their press-1 bridges PENDING_CALL_TTL_SEC how long a confirm-before-dial pending record lives: its TTL in seconds (default 120) |
| `VA_CALL_TIME_LIMIT_SEC` | `1800` | Optional spend caps (toll-fraud controls). Defaults shown. When the daily or the per-minute cap trips, the bot tells Zul and no call is placed. VA_CALL_DAILY_CAP max calls placed per rolling 24h (default 40, DB-backed by counting `call_audit`) VA_CALL_PER_MIN_CAP max triggers accepted per minute (default 5) VA_CALL_TIME_LIMIT_SEC per-call hard `timeLimit` on BOTH call legs (default 1800 = 30 min); both the lead-call and consult-call bridges reuse the same value, on their agent legs and on their press-1 bridges PENDING_CALL_TTL_SEC how long a confirm-before-dial pending record lives: its TTL in seconds (default 120) |
| `PENDING_CALL_TTL_SEC` | `120` | Optional spend caps (toll-fraud controls). Defaults shown. When the daily or the per-minute cap trips, the bot tells Zul and no call is placed. VA_CALL_DAILY_CAP max calls placed per rolling 24h (default 40, DB-backed by counting `call_audit`) VA_CALL_PER_MIN_CAP max triggers accepted per minute (default 5) VA_CALL_TIME_LIMIT_SEC per-call hard `timeLimit` on BOTH call legs (default 1800 = 30 min); both the lead-call and consult-call bridges reuse the same value, on their agent legs and on their press-1 bridges PENDING_CALL_TTL_SEC how long a confirm-before-dial pending record lives: its TTL in seconds (default 120) |
| `LEAD_CALL_ENABLED` |  | Lead call bridge (real-time first-ring on new Thumbtack leads). LEAD_CALL_ENABLED kill switch; `false` disables the new-Thumbtack-lead auto-call trigger entirely (redeploy-free, `HARVESTER_ENABLED` precedent). Default on: set `false` in Render BEFORE the feature's first deploy (ship dark until the live relay test passes). LEAD_CALL_DAILY_CAP max lead-call attempt chains opened per rolling 24h (default 25). Toll-fraud backstop for the lead call bridge; normal volume is ~2-3 leads/day. NOTE: `0` is NOT an off switch: `parseInt(...) \|\| 25` (the NaN-guard) treats it as unset and falls back to 25. The only off switch is `LEAD_CALL_ENABLED=false`. |
| `LEAD_CALL_DAILY_CAP` | `25` | Lead call bridge (real-time first-ring on new Thumbtack leads). LEAD_CALL_ENABLED kill switch; `false` disables the new-Thumbtack-lead auto-call trigger entirely (redeploy-free, `HARVESTER_ENABLED` precedent). Default on: set `false` in Render BEFORE the feature's first deploy (ship dark until the live relay test passes). LEAD_CALL_DAILY_CAP max lead-call attempt chains opened per rolling 24h (default 25). Toll-fraud backstop for the lead call bridge; normal volume is ~2-3 leads/day. NOTE: `0` is NOT an off switch: `parseInt(...) \|\| 25` (the NaN-guard) treats it as unset and falls back to 25. The only off switch is `LEAD_CALL_ENABLED=false`. |
| `CONSULT_CALL_ENABLED` |  | Consult call bridge (auto-dial Dallas at each Cal.com consult slot; spec 2026-08-25). CONSULT_CALL_ENABLED: the kill switch; only the literal `false` disables it. Off, the Cal.com webhook tail does nothing, the 60-second sweep opens, files and rings nothing, the ring-3 hop to Zul and the missed-chain text are refused (a client-no-answer report from a bridge already under way still goes out), answering a ring speaks the off message, "The consult call bridge is turned off", and press-1 dials nothing, and a ring 1, 2 or 3 that ends parks its chain `skipped_disabled`. The sweep's stale reap still runs while off, parking stranded chains `skipped_disabled` silently, with no email (spec 2026-09-30). Default on: set `false` in Render BEFORE the feature's first deploy, and flip it after the launch call test passes. CONSULT_CALL_DAILY_CAP: max consult ring chains opened per rolling 24h (default 10). Toll-fraud backstop; `0` is NOT off: it is treated as unset (`parseInt \|\| 10`, the NaN-guard) and falls back to 10, so the only off switch is `CONSULT_CALL_ENABLED=false`. The Cal.com booking page is PUBLIC, so this value sets THREE ceilings at once and you cannot raise one without raising the others. (a) Chains opened: the count excludes `status LIKE 'skipped%'`, counting only `status NOT LIKE 'skipped%'` (ruling R15), so a chain ending `skipped_cancelled` frees its slot again -- deliberate, because counting skipped rows would let a burst of junk bookings on the public page hold the whole feature down for 24 hours. That bounds the Manila legs and texts at this value. (b) Rings placed at `ADMIN_PHONE` in the same window: `dialCap()` = this value x `MAX_ADMIN_RINGS` (3), so 30 by default, counted from `call_audit` rows (status `consult_placed_admin`, one per ring actually placed) in the rolling 24h, and checked in `advanceChain`. (c) Legs placed to Zul: `vaLegCap()` = this value, counted the same way (`consult_placed_va`). The two leg ceilings count `call_audit` rows, one per leg actually placed, a ledger nothing else deletes (no chain transition or reschedule deletes it). They exist because the first ceiling alone left cancel-and-rebook unbounded: guard (b) exists because R15 left the book, ring, cancel, rebook cycle unbounded on the admin leg, and it is what makes the documented worst case a ceiling rather than a description. Raising this widens a public surface on all three, so treat any edit as a security change. CONSULT_CALLER_ID: strict E.164, the 1922 (the company line printed on proposals): the caller ID the client sees when Dallas pressed 1, so their callback rings the company line. FORMAT-checked, not presence-checked, at boot and again per call, because a typo would make every press-1 `<Dial>` fail at Twilio while the row already reads `connected`, and the only alert is the reaper's bridge-unconfirmed email, 40 minutes later. Unset or malformed falls back to `VOICE_CALLER_ID` (the 0082, Zul's line) with a boot warning; Zul's own press-1 always shows the 0082. RUN_CONSULT_CALL_SWEEP_SCHEDULER: optional; set to `false` to disable the 60-second consult call sweep (it opens a chain per upcoming Cal.com slot, files the slots that passed unrung, its missed windows, and places each due ring) AND the consult-call reaper it runs every tick (`consultCallReaper.js`: fails a chain stranded mid-ring 30 minutes past its slot, and flips a press-1 Twilio never reported to `failed` / `bridge_unconfirmed`). The stale half still runs with `CONSULT_CALL_ENABLED=false`, parking stranded chains silently. Setting THIS flag `false` leaves NO consult reaper at all, no rescue for a stranded chain, which is acceptable only while the feature is off. Default on. Honored only when `RUN_SCHEDULERS` is not `false`. |
| `CONSULT_CALL_DAILY_CAP` | `10` | Consult call bridge (auto-dial Dallas at each Cal.com consult slot; spec 2026-08-25). CONSULT_CALL_ENABLED: the kill switch; only the literal `false` disables it. Off, the Cal.com webhook tail does nothing, the 60-second sweep opens, files and rings nothing, the ring-3 hop to Zul and the missed-chain text are refused (a client-no-answer report from a bridge already under way still goes out), answering a ring speaks the off message, "The consult call bridge is turned off", and press-1 dials nothing, and a ring 1, 2 or 3 that ends parks its chain `skipped_disabled`. The stale half still runs with `CONSULT_CALL_ENABLED=false`, parking stranded chains silently. Setting THIS flag `false` leaves NO consult reaper at all, no rescue for a stranded chain, which is acceptable only while the feature is off. Default on. Honored only when `RUN_SCHEDULERS` is not `false`. |
| `RUN_CONSULT_CALL_SWEEP_SCHEDULER` |  | Consult call bridge (auto-dial Dallas at each Cal.com consult slot; spec 2026-08-25). CONSULT_CALL_ENABLED: the kill switch; only the literal `false` disables it. Off, the Cal.com webhook tail does nothing, the 60-second sweep opens, files and rings nothing, the ring-3 hop to Zul and the missed-chain text are refused (a client-no-answer report from a bridge already under way still goes out), answering a ring speaks the off message, "The consult call bridge is turned off", and press-1 dials nothing, and a ring 1, 2 or 3 that ends parks its chain `skipped_disabled`. The sweep's stale reap still runs while off, parking stranded chains `skipped_disabled` silently, with no email (spec 2026-09-30). Default on: set `false` in Render BEFORE the feature's first deploy, and flip it after the launch call test passes. CONSULT_CALL_DAILY_CAP: max consult ring chains opened per rolling 24h (default 10). Toll-fraud backstop; `0` is NOT off: it is treated as unset (`parseInt \|\| 10`, the NaN-guard) and falls back to 10, so the only off switch is `CONSULT_CALL_ENABLED=false`. The Cal.com booking page is PUBLIC, so this value sets THREE ceilings at once and you cannot raise one without raising the others. (a) Chains opened: the count excludes `status LIKE 'skipped%'`, counting only `status NOT LIKE 'skipped%'` (ruling R15), so a chain ending `skipped_cancelled` frees its slot again -- deliberate, because counting skipped rows would let a burst of junk bookings on the public page hold the whole feature down for 24 hours. That bounds the Manila legs and texts at this value. (b) Rings placed at `ADMIN_PHONE` in the same window: `dialCap()` = this value x `MAX_ADMIN_RINGS` (3), so 30 by default, counted from `call_audit` rows (status `consult_placed_admin`, one per ring actually placed) in the rolling 24h, and checked in `advanceChain`. (c) Legs placed to Zul: `vaLegCap()` = this value, counted the same way (`consult_placed_va`). The two leg ceilings count `call_audit` rows, one per leg actually placed, a ledger nothing else deletes (no chain transition or reschedule deletes it). They exist because the first ceiling alone left cancel-and-rebook unbounded: guard (b) exists because R15 left the book, ring, cancel, rebook cycle unbounded on the admin leg, and it is what makes the documented worst case a ceiling rather than a description. Raising this widens a public surface on all three, so treat any edit as a security change. CONSULT_CALLER_ID: strict E.164, the 1922 (the company line printed on proposals): the caller ID the client sees when Dallas pressed 1, so their callback rings the company line. FORMAT-checked, not presence-checked, at boot and again per call, because a typo would make every press-1 `<Dial>` fail at Twilio while the row already reads `connected`, and the only alert is the reaper's bridge-unconfirmed email, 40 minutes later. Unset or malformed falls back to `VOICE_CALLER_ID` (the 0082, Zul's line) with a boot warning; Zul's own press-1 always shows the 0082. RUN_CONSULT_CALL_SWEEP_SCHEDULER: optional; set to `false` to disable the 60-second consult call sweep (it opens a chain per upcoming Cal.com slot, files the slots that passed unrung, its missed windows, and places each due ring) AND the consult-call reaper it runs every tick (`consultCallReaper.js`: fails a chain stranded mid-ring 30 minutes past its slot, and flips a press-1 Twilio never reported to `failed` / `bridge_unconfirmed`). The stale half still runs with `CONSULT_CALL_ENABLED=false`, parking stranded chains silently. Setting THIS flag `false` leaves NO consult reaper at all, no rescue for a stranded chain, which is acceptable only while the feature is off. Default on. Honored only when `RUN_SCHEDULERS` is not `false`. |
| `TT_AUTOREPLY_ENABLED` |  | TT auto first-reply (respond-then-ring): the harvester box sends Dallas's saved day/night quick reply on every new lead; day-lead calls fire only after the reply is confirmed. TT_AUTOREPLY_ENABLED master switch, **DEFAULT OFF** (`'true'` to enable). Gates the webhook-tail fork (enqueue vs direct call) AND empties the agent offer endpoint. Deliberately does NOT gate the fallback sweep, so a rollback flip still drains in-flight day leads. FIRST_REPLY_FALLBACK_MINUTES the day-lead call fires anyway when the reply is unconfirmed past this fallback threshold (default 3 min) FIRST_REPLY_CALL_MAX_AGE_MINUTES the freshness bound on callback/sweep calls: no callback or sweep call ever fires for a lead older than it (default 240 min; the promise of a call expires) RUN_FIRST_REPLY_FALLBACK_SCHEDULER optional; `false` disables the 60s first-reply sweep (call fallback + strand hygiene). Default on. Honored only when `RUN_SCHEDULERS` is not `false` (the global flag wins). MAX_FIRST_REPLY_ATTEMPTS the offer-side attempts cap before a queued reply flips to `failed` (default 3; the OFFER bumps the counter, so a dead agent still hits the cap) FIRST_REPLY_COOLDOWN_INTERVAL the reply lease re-offer interval (default `'10 minutes'`) FIRST_REPLY_NIGHT_JITTER_START_HOUR / _END_HOUR the Chicago dead-hours window `[start, end)` in which night-template leads get the 2-14 min reply jitter; outside it night replies are immediate. Defaults 2 and 8: "night leads are fast until 2am" (2026-08-03). May wrap midnight (e.g. 23/6 = 11pm-6am); start == end disables the jitter entirely. |

| `FIRST_REPLY_CALL_DELAY_SECONDS` | `60` | Breathing room between the confirmed/failed first reply and the promised lead call (default 60s; 0 = immediate; clamped to 0-600). In-process timer; the 60s sweep (Arm A) is the restart backstop and defers flipped rows by delay+60s, so it never preempts the breathing room, and the `lead_id`-UNIQUE attempt open absorbs double-fires. A delay that would cross the 23:00 clamp fires immediately instead (never converts a placeable call into an after-hours skip). |

### Marketing sends (Resend)

| Variable | Value in .env.example | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `MARKETING_SEND_GAP_MS` | unset | Optional. Milliseconds between recipients in a marketing campaign send (`server/routes/marketingSend.js`, default 600). Resend's published limit is 2 requests/second; the send is paced SERIALLY rather than bursting, because a burst that trips the limit fails an arbitrary subset and leaves a partially-sent campaign nobody can resume confidently. Set it to `0` in tests. Raising it makes a 500-recipient send take proportionally longer in one open HTTP request. |
| `RESEND_DAILY_CAP` | unset | Optional. Resend's daily sending allowance (default 100, the free tier). Surfaced on the Marketing Overview as today's send budget and used to flag a moment whose audience will not fit in one day. NOTE the budget counts CAMPAIGN sends only (`email_sends`); the real allowance is also consumed by proposals, invoices and the lifecycle touches, so remaining is an upper bound. |

<!-- /gen:env -->

The frontend uses one build-time variable set in `client/.env.production`:
- `REACT_APP_API_URL` — absolute URL to the backend (e.g., `https://os-g7oa.onrender.com`)

│       │   │   └── sendResult.js # Formats a POST /api/comms/send result payload (the object SendModal hands to onComplete) into one per-channel-truth toast line. Channels the admin never selected are omitted; every attempted channel reports its real outcome, so a partial failure ("Emailed <EMAIL>. Text failed: ...") never hides behind a blanket success. Returns { hadFailure, level, message }: hadFailure true when any attempted channel failed (pick toast.error) level 'error' | 'success' | 'info' (same signal, enum form) Shared by every SendModal-consuming surface; single source of truth (a cross-lane duplicate of this helper was unified here on 2026-07-18).

Money convention (per server/db/schema.sql:478-487): - NUMERIC(10,2) DOLLARS: proposals.total_price/amount_paid/deposit_amount, service_packages.*_rate/*_fee, service_addons.rate, proposal_addons.rate, etc. - INTEGER CENTS: stripe_sessions.amount, proposal_payments.amount, invoices.amount_due/amount_paid Use fmt$2dp for dollar fields, fmt$fromCents for cents fields.
│       │   │   ├── nav.js    # Nav groups for the Admin OS sidebar. `badgeKey` / `badgeKeys` map to the /api/admin/badge-counts response shape; read them ONLY through navBadgeCount so every consumer (Sidebar, the phone More list, the phone tab bar) sums the same way. `adminOnly` hides the item from managers. Set it whenever the destination's API is the server's adminOnly (which rejects managers), so the sidebar never offers a manager a link that bounces them straight back out.

│       │   │   │   ├── dutyKinds.js # Admin-creatable duty kinds for the "Add duty line" control. MIRROR of server/utils/dutyLines.js DUTY_KIND_LABELS (EVENT kinds only - review money is never manually creatable), kept in sync manually, same convention as eventTypes.js / gratuityLabels.js server+client twins. Rendered duty lines always use the LABEL THE PAYLOAD CARRIES; this list exists only so the add-select can offer kinds before any line exists. out_of_area is deliberately NOT offered here: bonus money flows through the shift-level knob with its own cap and lock (spec §6), never free-typed. default_cents mirror the policy amounts (spec §2); hosted_supplies is a flat $50 (Dallas 2026-08-07; the old rate-based block is gone), same as derivation.
│       │   │   │   └── tipStatus.js # A tip's Status on the cross-staff ledger, derived from the row ALONE. There is no tip -> payout key: a shift's tips pool across the event's bartenders, and a late tip lands in today's period while keeping the original shift, so a per-tip "lands in" period cannot be made truthful from event_date (spec §3 override). First match wins, in this order.

│       │   │   │   ├── editorCore.js # The pieces of the proposal/event editor that the phone edit sheet (built in lane ma-e3 as client/src/components/mobile/useEditSheet.js) shares with the desktop editor (ProposalEditorForm.js). Moved out of ProposalEditorForm verbatim: one copy, so the two surfaces cannot drift. The other shared builders live beside this file: formState.js (the form a proposal seeds), patchBody.js (the save payload), repriceSummary.js (the booked-event reprice lines).
│       │   │   │   ├── formState.js # Shared form-state builders for the proposal/event editor (ProposalEditorForm). Moved verbatim from ProposalDetailEditForm.js so both mounts seed identically.
│       │   │   │   ├── patchBody.js # The ONE place a proposal-editor save payload is built. Both mounts of ProposalEditorForm (proposal page and event page) and the phone edit sheet (lane ma-e3) call this, so the surfaces cannot drift. History: the old EventEditForm built its own payload and omitted addon_quantities; the server defaults an absent quantity to 1 (safeAddonQty), so a date edit from the event page silently reset admin-set add-on quantities. Structural fix: one builder, always complete.
│       │   │   │   ├── repriceSummary.js # Pure decision + copy assembly for the booked-event reprice confirmation. Client-side PREDICTION of what PATCH /proposals/:id will do (crud.js: payment-status reconcile, additional-invoice creation, invoice refresh). It never becomes a second decision-maker: the server transaction is byte-identical whether or not the modal was shown.
