│       ├── styles/
│       │   └── drb-tokens.css # Dr. Bartender - Design System Tokens Midnight Ink chalkboard · Apothecary Teal · Antique Brass Aged-paper card surfaces · IM Fell typography
│       └── utils/
│           ├── addonRateLabel.js # The ONE qualified label for a `per_guest_timed` add-on rate.
│           ├── adjustmentRules.js # Client twin of server/utils/adjustmentRules.js: one definition of a price adjustment line (spec 2026-10-16 section 3). Keep the constants, the five readers and isWholeCents identical to the server's; server/utils/adjustmentRules.test.js imports this file and pins parity. adjVisible, adjAmount and adjLabel are the pricing engine's own coercions; adjSignedCents rounds per line (exact for whole-cent lines). isWholeCents is the server's whole-cents rule, so a client input can refuse what the server would. No validateAdjustments here: the server is the authority on what a saved line may hold.
│           ├── adminPush.js  # The admin phone's push state and switches (spec 2026-08-13-mobile-admin section 7, 2026-10-14). Thin over pushSubscribe.js: this file knows the admin worker's path and the rule that the switch shows this device's REAL state (Android's permission, then whether the admin registration holds a subscription), never a stored flag. The VAPID key comes from the API (GET /me/notification-preferences, push.public_key), not from the build.
│           ├── adminSw.js    # Register the admin service worker and speak its message protocol. Callers do not await anything useful from failure: an unregistered SW just means no offline shell this session.
│           ├── api.js        # Axios instance with JWT interceptor
│           ├── beoOutcomeCopy.js # One sentence for what the derived BEO finalize did after a shopping-list approve (the only derived trigger since 2026-09-22; approving the list IS the review). `beo` is the server's report ({ finalized, reason, unpaid_extras_cents }); the reason codes mirror server/utils/beoFinalize.js. `finalized` means "the BEO is finalized now" (the server reports it true on already_finalized too), so it wins over any reason. Empty string means "say nothing" (the action's own toast stands alone).
│           ├── buildTipDeepLink.js # Returns the URL to navigate to when the customer taps a payment button. Cash App and PayPal pre-fill the amount via URL; Venmo and Stripe ignore it.
│           ├── chicagoDay.js # The calendar day, in the business timezone, of a TIMESTAMPTZ moment.
│           ├── clientSources.js # Client acquisition source vocabulary (clients.source dropdown + badges). Mirrors server/utils/clientSources.js (CLIENT_SOURCES) - the canonical list. Keep both files in sync when adding a source, and also update: - server/routes/clients.js VALID_SOURCES validator - the clients_source_check CHECK in server/db/schema.sql (base def + migration) - the local `SOURCE` badge maps in client/src/pages/admin/ClientsDashboard.js and client/src/pages/admin/ClientDetail.js
│           ├── constants.js  # Shared constants - single source of truth for hardcoded business values
│           ├── consultCallLabel.js # Consult call bridge, the admin detail vocabulary (spec 2026-08-25 section 5.3). One line on ProposalDetail, one line per consult on ClientDetail, both fed by server/utils/consultCallLookups.js. Rows arrive snake_case, exactly as that module selects them.
│           ├── desktopViewStore.js # Per-screen "Desktop view" overrides for the phone admin (spec section 3: the no-dead-ends escape hatch). localStorage-persisted so the choice is a real exit that survives reloads; keyed per screen so opting one surface out of the phone treatment does not opt out all of them. Cleared on logout by purgeMobileAdminState() (lane ma-b; spec section 7 purge rule).
│           ├── downloadFilename.js # Filenames for browser-triggered downloads. Extracted from MenuPNG.jsx (2026-08-11) when the tip-sign download needed the same sanitizing; a second private copy is how two exports start disagreeing about what a legal filename is.
│           ├── downloadMenuPrint.js # Saves the bar menu print file from an authed API path (staff: /shifts/:shiftId/menu-print, admin: /proposals/:id/menu-print): the server's filename, a blob-type fallback, the deferred object-URL revoke
│           ├── downscaleImage.js # Browser-side photo downscaler run before upload
│           ├── drinkPlanStatusMap.js # Shared drink-plan status -> {label, kind} for admin StatusChips. Union of the two hand-copied maps (DrinkPlansDashboard + potions/PlansDrawer), including `exploration_saved` which the dashboard copy had dropped (it fell back to the raw enum string). Anchored on the PlansDrawer copy; DrinkPlansDashboard keeps its own `pending` = 'warn' attention affordance locally (the copies disagreed on that kind and the spec did not name a canonical for it).
│           ├── editSheetView.js # Pure view-model for the phone edit sheet (lane ma-e3; spec 2026-08-13-mobile-admin section 3, "Brainstorm decisions of 2026-10-05"; benchmark 2026-09-15, Edit details and the edit sheet). No React, no fetch. The money lines come from the desktop's own buildRepriceSummary, so both surfaces say the same thing from one copy.
│           ├── emailValidation.js # Warn-only heuristic flagging email domains one edit from a common TLD (.com/.net/.org) or a major mail provider. Not a format validator. Kept in sync manually with server/utils/emailValidation.js (same pattern as eventTypes.js ESM/CJS pair).
│           ├── eventCards.js # Groups the admin GET /shifts rows (one per shift) into one card per event for the phone Events list (spec 2026-08-13-mobile-admin section 4, benchmark 2026-09-15). Pure: no React, no fetch, no clock unless the caller passes one. Lane ma-e2 reuses the Card shape for the detail header.
│           ├── eventDetailView.js # Pure view-model for the phone event detail (spec 2026-08-13-mobile-admin section 4 Detail, benchmark 2026-09-15). No React, no fetch. Everything the screen shows is derived here from the four reads, so the rules are tested without rendering.
│           ├── eventTypes.js # Resolves an event type id to a human label. Mirrors server/utils/eventTypes.js - keep both files in sync when adding types.
│           ├── formatDelta.js # Shared change-request dollar-delta formatter (admin queue/card + public portal form)
│           ├── formatMoney.js # Format an integer-cents amount as a human dollar string.
│           ├── formatPhone.js # Phone number formatting
│           ├── gratuityLabels.js # Mirror of server/utils/gratuityLabels.js - keep VALUES + resolver branches byte-identical (synced manually, like eventTypes.js). server/utils/ gratuityLabels.test.js asserts parity. Canonical labels are payroll/back-compat load-bearing.
│           ├── hiddenRows.js # Which rows of an admin pricing breakdown are hidden from the client (spec 2026-10-16 section 4.5). The admin views (components/PricingBreakdown.js on the desktop, financialsOf in utils/eventDetailView.js on the phone) draw snapshot.breakdown, which lists every adjustment, shown or hidden, so each hidden line gets a "hidden from client" tag.
│           ├── installAdminPwaMeta.js # Inject the ADMIN-scoped PWA metadata at runtime. Sibling of installStaffPwaMeta.js, which stays untouched: the same built bundle serves admin, staff, hiring, and public, so each surface injects its own manifest behind its own host gate and the installs never collide (spec section 7). The FILENAME is load-bearing: scripts/sensitive-paths.txt lists it.
│           ├── installPrompt.js # Capture Chrome's beforeinstallprompt so the More tab can offer an explicit "Install app" row instead of depending on the browser banner's mood (spec section 7). Captured at module import: the event fires early, usually before any React mount.
│           ├── installStaffPwaMeta.js # Inject the staff-scoped PWA metadata at runtime.
│           ├── isPlaceholderEmail.js # Mirror of server/utils/emailValidation.js isPlaceholderEmail - keep in sync. RFC-2606 .invalid placeholders (CC import) are not real addresses: the server silently drops them, so the UI must treat them as no-email (no receipt popup, email channel unavailable).
│           ├── leadSources.js # Email-lead attribution vocabulary (email_leads.lead_source). Mirrors server/utils/clientSources.js (LEAD_SOURCES) - the canonical list. Keep both files in sync when adding a value, and also update: - server/routes/emailMarketing.js VALID_LEAD_SOURCES validator - the email_leads_lead_source_check CHECK in server/db/schema.sql (base def + migration) Consumers (filter dropdowns, edit forms, audience selector) pick up new values automatically.
│           ├── markdownBold.js # The ONE client-side reader of the `**bold**` runs our legal copy carries.
│           ├── marketingTags.js # Marketing tag vocabulary, ESM mirror of server/utils/marketingTags.js. Client and server bundles are separate, so these are kept in sync by hand, the same arrangement as eventTypes.js and gratuityLabels.js. The CHECK constraint `client_tags_tag_check` in schema.sql is the third copy. Change all three together; a server test pins all three against each other.
│           ├── messageTypes.js # Display-only labels for message_log rows. Server stores the raw machine message_type; this maps the known ones to friendly text and falls back to the stored subject line (then a humanized type) for auto-captured 'other' rows.
│           ├── mobileLock.js # Phone lock model (30-min background + expired-JWT decisions, enrolled/nudge flags, 401-claim handler registry; keys purge on logout)
│           ├── notificationsView.js # The Notifications screen's copy and state rules (spec 2026-08-13-mobile-admin section 3, "Design-session decisions of 2026-10-14", and section 7). Pure: no React, no network, so the words the screen shows are pinned by tests. Copy law: no em dashes; the typographic apostrophe where the benchmark draws one.
│           ├── offlineRead.js # offlineGet: api.get plus the X-Offline-Ok header. The admin service worker stores and stale-serves a read ONLY for a request that carries it (the identity read /api/auth/me aside); a caller sends it only if it renders the staleness line and takes no action on a cache-served copy
│           ├── packageIncludes.js # Shared interpolation for package_includes strings. The DB rows carry {hours}, {bartenders}, and {bartenders_s} tokens; every client-facing surface that renders the includes list must run them through here. Extracted verbatim from the inline copy in proposalView/ProposalView.js; consumers: ProposalView, admin ProposalDetail, admin EventDetailPage, portal PrescriptionTab. A null/undefined ctx value leaves its token untouched (visible braces beat silently rendering a wrong number).
│           ├── preferredName.js # PORT of server/utils/staffDisplayName.js + staffDisplayName.validate.js. The browser needs the live preview without a round trip per keystroke, and CRA cannot import from outside client/src. The server is the source of truth and validates every write regardless of what happens here.
│           ├── proposalCards.js # Turns one GET /proposals row into what the phone Proposals card shows (spec 2026-08-13-mobile-admin section 5; benchmark docs/design-artifacts/2026-10-14-mobile-admin-shell.dc.html, Proposals tab). Pure: no React, no fetch, no clock unless the caller passes one. Lane ma-f1.
│           ├── proposalRules.js # Shared proposal business rules - bundle logic, addon filtering, guardrails. Pure functions: no React, no state. Consumed by the public Quote Wizard and the admin cockpit (ProposalCreate.js). A CJS twin at server/utils/proposalRules.js re-validates these rules authoritatively; keep the two in sync manually (same discipline as eventTypes.js).
│           ├── proposalStatusMap.js # Shared proposal status -> {label, kind} for admin StatusChips. Single source for what was hand-copied (and had diverged) across ProposalDetail, ProposalsDashboard, ClientDetail, and AlternativesPanel. Union of all four copies: `completed` is a success state (green 'ok'), and both `archived` and `declined` are always present. Casing follows the sentence-case variant used on the proposal detail surface.
│           ├── pushSubscribe.js # Web-Push subscription helper for the staff portal (Phase 11, Task 53).
│           ├── rankDrinkMatches.js # Fuzzy ranking of a client-typed custom drink request against the admin drink lists, for the shopping list's "Match to existing" picker (components/ShoppingList/NeedsRecipeSection.jsx).
│           ├── routeRestore.js # Resume-where-I-left-off (spec section 9). The admin manifest's start_url is /events; on a cold standalone launch the shell restores the last recorded admin route instead. sessionStorage guards once-per-launch. Half-finished edit sheets are deliberately NOT restored; this is route-level only. recordRoute is only ever called from inside the AdminLayout route element, so /login and the auth/reset pages can never be recorded (spec section 9).
│           ├── screenKey.js  # Route -> screen-key mapping for the Desktop-view override store and the mobile header title. Detail screens get their own key so "desktop for this event page" does not drag the list along with it. Titles come from the nav LABELS, not the URL segment: /staffing is "Staff", /blog is "Lab Notes", and /email-marketing must never render as "Email-marketing".
│           ├── searchView.js # The phone global search's model (spec 2026-08-13-mobile-admin section 6 and the 2026-09-15 Global search line; benchmark docs/design-artifacts/2026-10-14-mobile-admin-shell.dc.html, Global search; lane ma-f3). Pure: GET /admin/search's groups in, what the screen draws and where each row leads out. Class names live in SearchPhone.js, which the class contract test reads; this file has none.
│           ├── sentryScrub.js # client/src/utils/sentryScrub.js
│           ├── servingLabels.js # Shared serving-type display labels (extracted from DrinkPlansDashboard so the Potions plans drawer and the drink-plans index render identically).
│           ├── setupTime.js  # Setup Time (client twin). Mirrors server/utils/setupTime.js - keep the two in sync manually (mirrored-util pattern, like eventTypes.js). The server util is canonical; this client copy only derives the display clock time for back-facing staff/admin pages. Setup time is back-of-house only - never render it on public proposal/invoice/website surfaces.
│           ├── shoppingListOwed.js # One predicate for every admin surface that turns a plan row into "shopping list owed" work: the Events Plan column (components/adminos/eventPlan.js), the overview prep queue (pages/admin/overview/PrepQueue.js), and the Potions drawer chip (pages/admin/potions/PlansDrawer.js). The Potions badge count applies the same rule server-side (server/routes/admin/settings.js).
│           ├── staffingRoles.js # Mirror of server/utils/staffingRoles.js + positionsNeeded.js + staffingClassification.js. Keep in sync manually (same dual-file pattern as eventTypes.js). The exact label 'Bartender' is load-bearing for payroll on the server; here it drives per-role fill, the request picker, and waitlist classification.
│           ├── staffingSheet.js # Pure staffing logic for the phone staffing card and the assignment sheet (spec 2026-08-13-mobile-admin section 4 Detail, benchmark 2026-09-15). No React, no fetch. It rides on staffingRoles.js, the same module the desktop ShiftDrawer classifies with, so a request is "waitlisted" on the phone exactly when it is on the desktop.
│           ├── staleTime.js  # Renders the offline staleness line (spec section 7). Device-local time by design: the line answers "how old is what I'm seeing", not a business timestamp, so the repo's Chicago-keyed convention does not apply here. Screen lanes render formatStaleTime(res.staleAt) inside the .m-stale element.
│           ├── stripNav.js   # Shared behavior for `.mob-strip` horizontal nav strips (portal tabs, staff account nav). The CSS lives in index.css under "mob-strip". Pattern per the mobile-fixes spec: hidden scrollbar + right-edge fade cue + scroll-snap, with the active item centered on mount/deep-link.
│           ├── tapGuard.js   # For a moment after a phone sheet closes, an invisible layer over the whole screen takes any tap (design pass 2026-10-06, lane ma-e3b). Without it, the second tap of a double tap on the sheet's footer or scrim lands on whatever was beneath: the event detail, its header, or the tab bar under the footer. It is appended to the document's body, so it outlives the sheet that asked.
│           ├── timeOptions.js # Generate 30-minute time slot options. @param {number} startHour - Inclusive start hour (24h, 0–23). Default 0. @param {number} endHour - Exclusive end hour (24h, 1–24). Default 24. Last slot is (endHour - 1):30. @returns {Array<{value: string, label: string}>} e.g. [{ value: "06:00", label: "6:00 AM" }, ...]
│           ├── tipCardMarks.js # Pure: given the server's computed tip-method tokens, return which payment marks a surface may show.
│           ├── uploadLimits.js # Client-side upload rules.
│           ├── userRoutes.js # Applicant-tier routes (/apply, /application-status) are mounted on hiring.drbartender.com and the admin app, but NOT on staff.drbartender.com (see App.js StaffSiteRoutes). An applicant who lands on staff.* (a bookmark, a stale link, a fresh login) would otherwise resolve to the staff catch-all '/', which RequirePortal rejects back to getHomePath('/application-status'), an infinite Navigate-replace loop that trips Safari's "replaceState more than 100x/10s" limiter (Sentry CLIENT-5 / CLIENT-6). Kick them cross-domain to the hiring portal where the route exists. A full page load (window.location) is required because Vercel cross-subdomain rewrites do not fire on client-side React Router navs.
│           └── webauthnClient.js # Passkey client (@simplewebauthn/browser wrap: registerPasskey, unlockWithPasskey, isPasskeySupported)
├── docs/
├── scripts/
│   ├── backfill-duty-lines.js # backfill-duty-lines.js - one-time ship backfill (spec 2026-08-06 §10.1).
│   ├── backfill-positions-needed.js # One-time backfill: re-derive shifts.positions_needed for upcoming events from the full paid roster (bartenders + banquet servers + barbacks), per-role shrink-capped so an approved-active assignment is NEVER dropped.
│   ├── board-write.sh        # board-write.sh: concurrency-safe writer for docs/build-board.md.

│   ├── cc-transfer-events.js # One-time CheckCherry EVENT TRANSFER (cc-import phase 3).
│   ├── check-claudemd-invariants.sh # Mechanical coverage check for the CLAUDE.md rewrite (workflow-redesign L8). For each invariant in claudemd-invariants.txt, the doc MUST match the paired regex. Coarse and necessary-not-sufficient (proves the rule's substance is present, not just a keyword); a consistency agent does the semantic check. Usage: bash scripts/check-claudemd-invariants.sh [path-to-CLAUDE.md] [--additive-against <ref>]
│   ├── check-css-palette-scope.js # check-css-palette-scope.js - mechanical backstop for the legacy-palette scope.
│   ├── check-file-size.js    # File-size guard. Two modes: --staged : ratchet check for the pre-commit hook. A file over the hard cap fails ONLY if this commit makes it longer than it is at HEAD. --all : full-tree RED / YELLOW report. Always exits 0. Thresholds, scope, and line counting are defined once and shared by both.
│   ├── claudemd-invariants.txt

│   ├── content-rule-calibrate.js # content-rule-calibrate: a read-only report on the content rule in scripts/sensitive-match.js (instruction rewrite spec 2026-10-19, 3.3; step 2 lane sensitive-match-2). Two questions:
│   ├── dev-signin.js         # Dev sign-in for interactive local review (Playwright MCP, or any Playwright context). Mints a short-lived dev JWT for one account and writes it into a Playwright init script that plants the token in localStorage on that account's local dev host only. Prints the file's path, never the token.
│   ├── gen-docs.js           # gen-docs.js: writes and checks the generated sections of README.md and ARCHITECTURE.md from the code (instruction rewrite spec 2026-10-19 section 5.1; plan 2026-10-27 step 4, lane gen-docs, decisions 4, 13 and 14). Six sections, each between `<!-- gen:<name> -->` and `<!-- /gen:<name> -->`: tree, scripts and env in README.md (the folder tree from file headers, package.json's scripts, .env.example); routes, schema and agents in ARCHITECTURE.md (the route mounts, server/db/schema.sql, the .claude agents, commands and skills). Prose outside the markers is never touched, and the generator never creates a marker. `npm run docs` maps here.
│   ├── gmail-probe.js        # One-shot smoke of the Gmail feed key (spec 2026-10-14 section 10), the only Gmail call allowed from the dev box. Run it once, before the key goes into Render, with the key in THIS command's environment only. Reading the JSON file keeps the base64 out of shell history:
│   ├── guard-os-main.sh

It stays on main after its run as the record of what the migration did (decision 17).

│   ├── post-ship-watch.js    # The post-ship watch (instruction rewrite step 3, lane post-ship-watch; spec 2026-10-19 sections 3.11 and 4.4, plan 2026-10-22 decision 5 and the post-ship-watch.js contract, which binds; this header says what this file does and never restates the ship flow).
│   ├── push-gate.js          # push-gate: the push gate's CLI. `sha <sha>` gates one commit in a throwaway worktree and writes its receipt, `receipt <sha>` prints that receipt, and `verify`, the pre-push hook, validates the receipts and never runs a gate.

Paid proposals are NEVER touched. node scripts/reset-unpaid-gratuity.js # dry run (default) node scripts/reset-unpaid-gratuity.js --apply # write
│   ├── review-brief.js       # review-brief.js: the one brief every lens of a review reads (instruction rewrite spec 2026-10-19, section 3.4; plan 2026-10-20 step 2, lane review-tooling, the contract "The brief"). │   ├── review-select.js      # review-select.js: the tier and the lenses a review runs, from the diff (instruction rewrite spec 2026-10-19, sections 3.2 and 3.3; plan 2026-10-20 step 2, lane review-tooling). Consumers: scripts/lane-status.js today; the review skill (scripts/review-select.js), merge-lane.sh check and ship from steps 2 to 5.

│   │   ├── devDbGate.js      # Dev-only gate shared by the scripts that mint dev JWTs against the dev branch (scripts/dev-signin.js, scripts/mobile-capture.js). Call it BEFORE any DB connection or token minting; `die` prints and exits.
│   │   ├── flockExec.js      # flockExec.js: run the calling script under an exclusive flock(1), shared by scripts/run-suites.js (the dev-suite lock) and scripts/testdb-smoke.js (the smoke lock). Instruction rewrite step 2, lane suite-runner.
│   │   ├── gatePaths.js      # The gate's path constants and its range classifier, a leaf module (instruction rewrite step 3, lane ship-state; spec 2026-10-19 sections 4.3 and 4.7, plan 2026-10-22 decision 18). push-gate.js held MONEY_PATHS and CLIENT_PATHS until this step; from the gate-sha lane on it re-exports them from here, so its tests keep the names they import, and the sha gate, rangeCheck and ship's manifest all classify a range through touches(). This file requires only sensitive-match.js and receipt.js, never push-gate.js or rangeCheck.js, so no require cycle can form through it.
│   │   ├── lensTriggers.js   # Lens selection (instruction rewrite spec 2026-10-19, sections 3.2 and 3.3; plan 2026-10-20 step 2, lane review-tooling, the contract "Lens selection"). A review runs the tier floors plus every lens whose trigger the diff matches. The triggers are named, <lens>.<slug>; each check in a lens file under .claude/agents/ ends its trigger line with the names that point at it, and the brief's section "Triggers that fired for you" lists the names that fired, so a lens reads those checks first. Consumers: scripts/review-select.js (selectLenses, forecastDiff) and scripts/review-brief.js (the names).
│   │   ├── markers.js        # The flip marker and the two push markers (instruction rewrite step 3, lane ship-state; spec 2026-10-19 section 4.4, plan 2026-10-22 decisions 1 and 16).
│   │   ├── mergeCheck.js     # The merge path's receipt check and the merged-lane checks (instruction rewrite step 5, lane merge-check; spec 2026-10-19 sections 3.7, 3.8, 4.1, 4.6 and D2; plan 2026-10-28 decisions 8 and 22). merge-lane.sh check and merged run its CLI; checkLane is the check the ship phase re-runs (lane merge-ship).
│   │   ├── mergeShip.js      # The merge script's ship phase: merge-lane.sh ship checks a lane's receipt under the ship lock, squashes the tip the check judged through the shell's own squash, writes the squash receipt and ships it through shipFlow, in the background (instruction rewrite step 5, lane merge-ship; spec 2026-10-19 sections 3.7, 3.8, 3.11, 4.1, 4.3, 4.4 and D10; plan 2026-10-28 decisions 8, 14, 18 and 22).
│   │   ├── mergeShipLog.js   # The ship phase's own records (instruction rewrite step 5, lane merge-ship): the run log merge-lane.sh ship writes, its stdio sibling and the phase manifest, written by scripts/lib/mergeShip.js; scripts/lane-status.js reads the manifests through phaseLines. No CLI.
│   │   ├── mergeShipSquash.js # The squash's proofs and records for the ship phase (instruction rewrite step 5, lane merge-ship), split from scripts/lib/mergeShip.js, whose header is the contract these serve (the run's steps 1 to 5, the resume and --resolved): the refusals before anything is merged (preRefusal, with the comment-only lane's squash keyed ahead, squashKeyAhead; and markerRefusal, shipFlow's precheck and the marker refusals, which a resume shares), --resolved's index rules and proof (resolvedIndexProblem, verifyResolved, indexTree, differsOutsideDocs, wasReset), the run directory and the shell's squash (writeRunDir, squashShell, with the shell's first REFUSED line, refusalOf), the squash on main (findSquash), a lane's squash on main and not yet pushed (unpushedOf and findUnpushed: merged's first check, the subject opening merge(lane <branch>): , over origin/main..main, a squash a later commit reverts not resumable; resumeProblem, the squash receipt's lane tip against the branch's before a resume, and strandLine, the branch's tree against the squash's, the two docs by their prose (proseDiffers), before a resume and before the branch delete, which the cleanup ties to the tip it read, dropAt; the cleanup's branch step, branchOutcome; and removeLaneWorktree), the squash receipt (writeSquashReceipt) and the ready-moved re-run of the receipt's suites on the merged tree (suitesStep, the shell's callback through mergeShip.js suites), with the git reads the run shares (worktreeOf; branchInUse, a worktree holding the branch in a rebase or a bisect, which mergeCheck's merged-lane worktree check reads too; wedged). No CLI of its own.
│   │   ├── mergeShipTestkit.js # The end to end harness of the merge-lane.sh ship suites (scripts/merge-lane.ship.test.js and its siblings; plan 2026-10-28 step 5, lane merge-ship): a throwaway os under the temp dir with a local bare origin and the lanes in linked worktrees under its ../worktrees/, carrying its own copy of scripts/ (out of git through .git/info/exclude, but for the sensitive list, which main holds), so the locks, the markers, the receipts and the ship records are all its own; the flip marker written in its common dir unless flip is false; real receipts through the review-receipt CLI (scripts/lib/receiptTestkit.js); the sha gate and the watch as stubs named by DRB_SHIP_GATE_CMD and DRB_SHIP_WATCH_CMD, each recording its runs; HOME a temp dir. The copy holds no scripts/gen-docs.js unless genDocs is set: then a stand-in (GEN_DOCS) whose --generated-only always says the change is inside the generated sections and whose --staged writes one marked line into the index's README.md and stages it, as step 7's regeneration rewrites the docs: inside its tree section when it has one (the real generator writes nothing outside its sections), else at its end. A suite sets TMPDIR to its own temp dir before its first case. Nothing here touches os, its origin or the dev database.
│   │   ├── piiShapes.js      # piiShapes: the one PII shape module (instruction rewrite spec 2026-10-19, 3.3 and 3.10; D9). Three consumers read it so the shapes cannot drift: the board writer (scripts/board-write.sh --check), the static pre-step (scripts/static-checks.js, the `pii` rule over added lines) and the second-opinion mask that runs before a diff leaves the box for codex or gemini.
│   │   ├── rangeCheck.js     # The receipt check over a push range (instruction rewrite step 3, lane range-check; spec 2026-10-19 sections 3.7, 3.10, 4.7, 4.8 and D2; plan 2026-10-22 decisions 8, 9 and 18). ship and the pre-push hook call the one function, checkRange({base, tip, cwd}), and refuse on any problem.
│   │   ├── receipt.js        # The review receipt (instruction rewrite step 2, lane receipt-lib; spec 2026-10-19 sections 3.5 and 3.7). The keys, the verdict-table parser (receiptVerdicts.js, re-exported), the receipt directory and its index, and the commit-message block. The review skill writes receipts through scripts/review-receipt.js; steps 3 and 5 read them through this module, so the parser merge-lane.sh and ship trust is the one the skill wrote with.
│   │   ├── receiptBase.js    # finalize's judgment of the commits and the bases (instruction rewrite step 2, lane receipt-lib; spec 2026-10-19 sections 3.7 and 3.8), as pure functions, moved out of scripts/lib/receiptFinalize.js, which runs them in validate(): the commits against each other (checkCommits), each report against the base (checkMains), the overlap pass of a review rebased onto a newer main (checkOverlap), and what changed since a report was read (changedFor, which the stale rule reads). Nothing here touches the disk or git; the facts are the ones scripts/lib/receiptGit.js gathers (receiptFinalize.js's header names them). A problem names commits, lenses, files and counts, never a word of a report.
│   │   ├── receiptExternals.js # Recording the second opinion's reviewers into a review's working directory (instruction rewrite step 2, lane receipt-lib; scripts/review-receipt.js `externals`). Each argument is one reviewer's state: codex=run, codex=run:<output file>, codex=not_run:<reason>, or second-opinion.md's fixed line as it prints it, codex: run (<note>) or codex: not run (<reason>); the two fixed lines may come as one argument, a line each. An entry carries the position snapshot it was recorded at, when there is one: recorded, never gated (spec 3.10). A message names reviewers and states only, never a note, a reason or a line of an output file.
│   │   ├── receiptFinalize.js # finalize's validation (instruction rewrite step 2, lane receipt-lib; spec 2026-10-19 sections 3.5 to 3.9), as pure functions. scripts/review-receipt.js reads the files and asks git, and hands the results here; nothing in this module touches the disk or git. validate() returns every problem, one line each, and the receipt.json finalize would write; a problem names lenses, files, ids and counts, never a word of a report, a log or a reason. The commits and the bases are judged in scripts/lib/receiptBase.js.
│   │   ├── receiptGit.js     # The git facts a review reads (instruction rewrite step 2, lane receipt-lib; scripts/review-receipt.js): the diff's files as git sees them now (previewFiles, for the coverage line verdicts prints), and everything finalize judges (finalizeFacts), handed to scripts/lib/receiptFinalize.js, which judges and never asks git itself. Nothing here writes but the tree object git write-tree stores for the quick-fix key.
│   │   ├── receiptPosition.js # The position snapshot (instruction rewrite step 2, lane receipt-lib; scripts/review-receipt.js `position`). A verdict holds for the content its lens read, so a stored report records where the lens's input was taken, not where the branch stood when the report came back: `position <name>` runs right before the input is taken (review-brief runs it when it writes the diff; until then the skill runs it by hand) and again before any re-dispatch, and writes <work dir>/position.json: seq one more than the highest of the last snapshot's seq and the position of every stored report, test entry (superseded too) and external, so a lost or replaced position.json never reuses a seq a stored entry carries; 1 for the first tip the branch tip (lane), HEAD (quick fix), the commit (commit review) tree the staged tree, git write-tree (quick fix; null otherwise) head the checkout's HEAD (commit review only): its tests run on the commit's own tree, so a test entry is stored only while HEAD, at the snapshot and at the store, is the commit main refs/heads/main overlap the main commit this snapshot is the overlap pass against (`position <name> --overlap <sha>`, spec 3.8; the skill declares it, review-brief passes it), a commit on main; null otherwise. finalize --stale-base counts a lens's report as the overlap pass only when it was stored under a snapshot whose overlap is --reviewed-against at when it was taken `position` prints `position <seq> tip <sha7>`; the skill (and review-brief) passes the seq through, and verdicts, verify and tests take it as the required --position <seq>. A report, a verify report or a test entry stored later carries tip, tree, main, overlap and head (when set) and position (the seq) from the snapshot. position replaces an unreadable position.json, saying so in one stderr line. The store is refused when there is no snapshot (or a quick fix's records no staged tree); when the seq it was read at is not the current snapshot's (position ran again before it was stored, so it would be recorded at a position its lens never read); or when the branch tip (a quick fix's HEAD or staged tree) is no longer the snapshot's: something moved under the lens, and its verdict cannot be pinned to either position.
│   │   ├── receiptPublish.js # Publishing a finalized receipt (instruction rewrite step 2, lane receipt-lib; scripts/review-receipt.js `finalize`). The receipt is built in a fresh directory beside its key (a copy of the working directory plus the failing log, the proof, externals/not-run.txt and the final receipt.json), renamed into place, then the working directory is removed. The working receipt.json is never marked final, so a failure before the rename removes the partial directory and leaves the review open for another finalize (exit 1, one line); a failure after it, in the index, exits 3.
│   │   ├── receiptTestkit.js # The shared fixtures of the review-receipt CLI suites (instruction rewrite step 2, lane receipt-lib): throwaway repos under the temp dir, the CLI run against them through REVIEW_RECEIPT_ROOT, lens reports, TAP logs, runner directories and select.json files. Requiring it scrubs the git variables a hook would set and keeps the user's git config out, so a suite proves the receipt pins its own flags. Nothing here touches the dev database.
│   │   ├── receiptTests.js   # Recording a test run into a review's working directory (instruction rewrite step 2, lane receipt-lib; scripts/review-receipt.js `tests`). Each function takes the working directory and its state, copies what it read into tests/, and returns the entry the CLI appends to state.tests; a run it cannot vouch for throws, and nothing is copied.
│   │   ├── receiptVerdicts.js # The verdict table of a lens report (instruction rewrite step 2, lane receipt-lib; spec 2026-10-19 section 3.5), re-exported by scripts/lib/receipt.js. Pure: text in, rows and findings out.
│   │   ├── reviewBriefEstablished.js # The Established section of the review brief (instruction rewrite step 2, lane review-tooling; scripts/review-brief.js renders it, split out in fix pass 3 to keep that file under its size line). It carries the pre-step result, the code-path sections diff.patch shows only as Binary files lines, the tests run with their counts and logs, the client build, what the tiers require, and the questions already settled.
│   │   ├── shaGate.js        # shaGate: the push gate's sha mode, a leaf module (instruction rewrite step 3, lane gate-sha; spec 2026-10-19 sections 4.3 and 4.4, plan 2026-10-22 decisions 2, 3, 4, 10, 11, 16 and 18). push-gate.js keeps the CLI, the lock and the modes, and re-exports classifyRange, checkShaReceipt, gateEnvFile and gateWorktreePath from here. This file requires only gatePaths.js, markers.js, worktreeLinks.js, receipt.js, shaGateWorktree.js (the worktree and child half) and Node's own modules, never push-gate.js or rangeCheck.js, so no require cycle can form through it.
│   │   ├── shaGateWorktree.js # shaGateWorktree: the sha gate's worktree and child half, a leaf module (instruction rewrite step 3, lane gate-sha; plan 2026-10-22 decision 11). shaGate.js requires it for the paths, the gate worktree's removal and sweep, the smoke logs it keeps, the children it runs and stops, and the probe that says whether the docs check applies (step 5, lane gate-markers). This file requires only receipt.js and Node's own modules.
│   │   ├── shipFlow.js       # shipFlow: the ship flow, the leaf scripts/ship.js runs under the ship lock (instruction rewrite step 3, lane ship; spec 2026-10-19 sections 4.1 to 4.5 and 4.8; plan 2026-10-22 decisions 6, 8, 9, 10, 12, 16 and 18). The flow's order and its endings are the plan's ship.js contract as amended (docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-22-instruction-rewrite-step3.md, "Contracts shared across lanes" and "Amendments (as built)"); this header says what the module guarantees and never restates them. ship.js holds the CLI, the lock and the detached start; this file requires only scripts/lib modules and never ship.js.
│   │   ├── shipLog.js        # The ship log and its manifest (instruction rewrite step 3, lane ship-state; spec 2026-10-19 sections 4.1 and 4.4, plan 2026-10-22 decision 6).
│   │   ├── tapSummary.js     # The one TAP summary parser (instruction rewrite step 2, lane suite-runner). scripts/run-suites.js, scripts/testdb-smoke.js and the review receipt all read their counts here, from the summary block Node's TAP reporter ends a run with (`1..N`, `# tests N`, `# pass N`, `# fail N`, ...), never from an exit code alone. A log with no such block is `found: false`: a failure, never a zero.
│   │   ├── worktreeLinks.js  # worktreeLinks: the one list of symlinks a worktree of this repo is born with, and the link that gives a lane window the main memory. Instruction rewrite step 3, lane worktree-links (spec 2026-10-19, sections 4.3 and 6.5; plan 2026-10-22, decision 15).
│   │   ├── genDocs/
│   │   │   ├── agents.js     # The agents, commands and skills tables for ARCHITECTURE.md's gen:agents section (instruction rewrite step 4, lane gen-docs; plan 2026-10-27 decision 4 and the contract "The agents table").
│   │   │   ├── env.js        # The environment-variable table for README.md's gen:env section, read from .env.example (instruction rewrite step 4, lane gen-docs; plan 2026-10-27 decision 6 and the contract "The env table").
│   │   │   ├── headers.js    # The header rule (instruction rewrite step 4, lane gen-docs; plan 2026-10-27 decision 1 and the contract "The header rule"). One reading of "a file's header comment" for the generated tree, the pre-commit check, the route walker's handler descriptions and the migration that writes headers.
│   │   │   ├── jsAst.js      # The JavaScript syntax helpers the route walker reads modules with (step 4, lane gen-docs; split out of routes.js to keep it under the 700-line cap). Every helper takes acorn's ESTree nodes and never runs the code.
│   │   │   ├── markers.js    # The generated-section markers and the text rules every generated section follows (instruction rewrite step 4, lane gen-docs; plan 2026-10-27 decision 4 and the contract "The markers").
│   │   │   ├── routes.js     # The route table for ARCHITECTURE.md's gen:routes section: a static parse of server/index.js and the router modules it mounts, never a require (step 4, lane gen-docs; plan 2026-10-27 decision 7, the contract "The route table"). scripts/route-inventory.js, the runtime walker, is its oracle, mount by mount.
│   │   │   ├── schema.js     # The schema index for ARCHITECTURE.md's gen:schema section, read from server/db/schema.sql (instruction rewrite step 4, lane gen-docs; plan 2026-10-27 decision 9 and the contract "The schema index"). It parses the file and never requires server/db/index.js, which opens a pool at load.
│   │   │   ├── scriptsTable.js # The npm scripts table for README.md's gen:scripts section (instruction rewrite step 4, lane gen-docs; plan 2026-10-27 decision 5 and the contract "The scripts table").
│   │   │   ├── source.js     # git's view of the tree the docs generator reads (instruction rewrite step 4, lane gen-docs; plan 2026-10-27 decision 13 and the contract "The sources"). The generator never reads the bare working tree for its file list, so a pathspec commit's temporary index, a lane's own index and a commit all read right: every call is git itself, which honors GIT_INDEX_FILE.
│   │   │   └── tree.js       # The generated folder tree for README.md's gen:tree section (instruction rewrite step 4, lane gen-docs; plan 2026-10-27 decision 3 and the contract "The tree").
│   │   ├── migrate/
│   │   │   ├── archRows.js   # ARCHITECTURE.md's hand route tables, read for the one-shot migration (instruction rewrite step 4, lane migrate-headers; plan 2026-10-27 decision 8 and the contract "scripts/migrate-headers.js"), and the comment writer the three migration commands share.
│   │   │   ├── envTables.js  # The two hand environment-variable tables (README.md's and the project CLAUDE.md's), read for the port into .env.example (instruction rewrite step 4, lane migrate-headers; plan 2026-10-27 decision 6, the contract "scripts/migrate-headers.js"): the draft the env-port lane edits, and the row-by-row check that the port kept every row.
│   │   │   └── readmeTree.js # README's hand folder tree, read for the one-shot header migration (instruction rewrite step 4, lane migrate-headers; plan 2026-10-27 decisions 2 and 3, the contract "scripts/migrate-headers.js"), and the header text a header-less source file gets.
│   │   └── staticChecks/
│   │       ├── envDefaults.js # envDefaults: the env-default reader behind the secret-default and env-default rules of scripts/lib/staticChecks/staticRules.js, over the espree tokens of scripts/lib/staticChecks/jsScan.js.
│   │       ├── eslintUndef.js # eslintUndef: the eslint-undef rule of scripts/static-checks.js. eslint through the repo's own binary and config, over the changed .js files under server/ and scripts/ (the client is covered by its CI build), with an override that reports only no-undef and no-use-before-define, as errors. The override strips every other rule, gives scripts/ the server block's language options (the repo config has no block for scripts/, where eslint's defaults would flag every require) plus the Node globals scripts use, and sets no-use-before-define to same-scope reads only: a const, let or class read before its line in its own scope is the TDZ family the lens audit recorded (D0491, D2469), while a hoisted function declaration (121 of the 125 default-option hits on the tree) and a module constant read from inside a function that runs later (the rest, and six in piiShapes.test.js) cannot throw. no-undef catches the dropped import (D0645, D0739).
│   │       ├── jsScan.js     # jsScan: the code view of a JS file for scripts/static-checks.js, built from espree's tokens. espree is eslint's own parser; it is resolved from the repo's node_modules (the reviewed repo first, then this script's own), and runs with JSX on and ecmaVersion latest, reading each file both as a script (commonjs) and as a module, the one eslint uses for it preferred (see scanJs). When neither tokenizes, or both do and disagree, scanJs throws and the caller fails closed: the file gets a blocking `scan` finding and no rule runs on it.
│   │       ├── packageJson.js # packageJson: the package.json reader behind the floating-security-versions and file-or-git-deps rules of scripts/lib/staticChecks/staticRules.js. The two package rules read the parsed JSON, so a key outside the dependency sections (repository, scripts) never counts and a "//" key is just a key.
│   │       └── staticRules.js # staticRules: the per-file rules of scripts/static-checks.js, the allow marker and checkFile. A rule's check returns candidate lines; the engine keeps those on an added line (a span counts when any line of it was added), except for a whole-file rule.
│   └── override-conversion/
│       ├── applyBlock.js     # Runs a rendered conversion block over pg against a conversion BRANCH: the dev rehearsal's forward and reverse runs (plan 2026-10-16-price-override-removal, Task V4). Prod's forward block runs through Neon MCP run_sql (runbook step 6), never through this script.
│       ├── branch.js         # The override conversion's Neon branch (plan 2026-10-16-price-override-removal, Task V1 step 1; spec section 8.5). The conversion reads a fresh branch, never prod itself, and never the push gate's ci-smoke branch.
│       ├── confirm.js        # The conversion's confirmation (plan 2026-10-16-price-override-removal, Task V4 runbook step 7; spec 8.5). Read-only, over pg, against a branch cut AFTER the forward block ran (on the rehearsal, the rehearsal branch itself), so no data passes through a chat.
│       ├── doBlock.js        # The override conversion's forward and reverse DO blocks (plan 2026-10-16-price-override-removal, Task V3; spec 8.5, decision 10).
│       ├── export.js         # The override conversion's export (plan 2026-10-16-price-override-removal, Task V1 step 2; spec 8.5, decision 10): reads a Neon BRANCH over pg and writes JSON (chmod 600) to the session scratchpad. Nothing passes through an MCP call or a chat.
│       ├── export.sql        # The override conversion's export (plan 2026-10-16-price-override-removal, Task V1 step 2; spec section 8.5). Two statements, run by export.js in ONE read-only REPEATABLE READ transaction against a Neon branch, never prod itself. Ids only first; the legal-hold seam drops held ids in export.js before the second statement, so no held booking's row is ever read. No names, emails or phones.
│       ├── fixtures.js       # Synthetic fixture rows for the override conversion's DB tests and its dev rehearsal (plan 2026-10-16-price-override-removal, Tasks V1, V3, V4). Synthetic labels and amounts only; every row hangs off one synthetic client and one synthetic package, and cleanup deletes them in foreign-key order. Never used against prod; the callers refuse NODE_ENV=production.
│       ├── freshCompute.js   # The fresh compute for live override bookings (plan 2026-10-16-price-override-removal, Task V1 step 3; spec 8.2): each live booking priced at TODAY's catalog with its current inputs and no override, through computeCatalogSnapshot (proposalExtrasFold.js, the fold's own engine-args builder).
│       ├── guard.js          # Runbook steps 1 and 5: the deployed-code guard on its own (shared.js assertDeployedCode; freshCompute.js and run.js also run it first). Fetches, then exits 0 only when server/ equals origin/main and the conversion tooling is committed; prints the origin/main sha to match against Render's live deploy. node scripts/override-conversion/guard.js
│       ├── rehearsal-seed.js # Seeds one synthetic booking of every conversion shape on a conversion branch, for the dev rehearsal (plan 2026-10-16-price-override-removal, Task V4).
│       ├── reverse.js        # A reverse block for PART of a conversion run (plan 2026-10-16-price-override-removal, lane conversion-tooling fix round). When a converted row moves after the forward block (a payment, an edit), the run's full reverse.sql refuses and restores nothing. This renders the reverse for the other rows under the run's own tag, so each still finds its activity record; the moved rows are settled by hand.
│       ├── run.js            # The override conversion's dry run (plan 2026-10-16-price-override-removal, Task V3; spec 8.5): the export and the fresh compute in; the change set, the report and both DO blocks out, all chmod 600 in the scratchpad (they carry free-text labels; the repo is public, so none of it is ever committed).
│       ├── shared.js         # Pieces the override conversion scripts share (plan 2026-10-16-price-override-removal, Tasks V1-V4).
│       └── transform.js      # The override conversion's transform (plan 2026-10-16-price-override-removal, Task V2; spec 8.2-8.4). Pure: export rows and the fresh compute in, the change set, the dry-run report and Dallas's lists out. Every compare is in integer cents; the engine's own coercions read every line (adjustmentRules).

│   │   ├── corsOptions.js    # Browser-facing CORS policy. Lives here rather than inline in server/index.js so it can be exercised without booting the app (index.js calls start() at load). index.js still owns the origin allowlist; this owns everything else.
│   │   └── rateLimiters.js   # Shared express-rate-limit instances (publicLimiter, signLimiter, adminWriteLimiter for admin proposal writes, menuDraftReadLimiter and menuDraftEditLimiter for the menu art drafts, etc.)

│   │   ├── adminCoverSwaps.js # Admin one-click cover-swap routes (spec section 6.5).

│   │   ├── blog.js           # Blog post endpoints
│   │   ├── calcom.js         # Cal.com webhook receiver (HMAC-SHA256 signed, public); handles booking created/cancelled/rescheduled/no-show events
│   │   ├── calendar.js       # Calendar/scheduling endpoints

Every admin-click client send routes through here: preview shows the live-resolved recipient + drafted message, send runs the action's idempotent side effects then dispatches with the admin's edits. The recipient is resolved server side ONLY - the request cannot override the destination.

Admin or manager, the admin write limiter (10 a minute per user). Never throws over a push failure: the counts are the answer.

Pricing that bills clients lives in pricingEngine/proposals - nothing here touches money paths.

│   │   ├── potions.js        # Potions (bar program) API: par catalog CRUD + reorder + shopping-list preview, plus the shared recipe-row validator the cocktails/mocktails routers consume (exported alongside the router, drinkPlanConsult precedent). Spec: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-09-potions-bar-program-design.md §4.
│   │   ├── progress.js       # Onboarding step tracking
│   │   ├── publicFeedback.js # Post-event feedback router (5-star sentiment routing)
│   │   ├── publicReviews.js  # Public cached endpoint for Thumbtack reviews on homepage
│   │   ├── publicTip.js      # Public tip-page lookup + post-tip feedback (token-gated)
│   │   ├── shifts.approval.js # Request -> approval money seam, extracted from shifts.js (which hit the 1000-line hard cap). Three handlers: a staffer requests a shift (ranked roles), an admin manually assigns, and an admin approves/denies a request. `position` is the only money-sensitive field here - payroll's tip split keys on LOWER(position) = 'bartender' - so it is resolved from the staffer's ranked requested_positions (or an explicit admin override) at approval time, never defaulted. shifts.js mounts these on its router with the shared auth / requireStaffing / requireOnboarded middleware.
│   │   ├── shifts.handlers.js # Shift-lifecycle mutation handlers, extracted from shifts.js (which sits under the 1000-line hard cap). Two bulky route bodies live here: the shift UPDATE (PATCH-semantic PUT /:id with equipment validation + BEO-cancel transaction) and the first-class cancel / unassign action. Money-adjacent (proposal->shift sync; staffing seam) - cancel-or-unassign denies approved shift_requests and suppresses BEO nudges. shifts.js mounts these on its router with the shared auth / requireStaffing middleware. EQUIPMENT_TOKENS is the closed logistics set, owned by shifts.approval.js and reused here for the PUT validation.
│   │   ├── shifts.js         # Shift scheduling
│   │   ├── shifts.queries.js # Long SQL strings for server/routes/shifts.js (Task 28 projections).
│   │   ├── sms.js            # Twilio inbound-SMS webhook (line-aware), the message status callback (POST /status), and the admin thread API; the status callback and the Messages reply clear the Inbox cache, and a first reply from a 224 line takes the Inbox first-text prefix
│   │   ├── smsOptIn.js       # Standalone public SMS opt-in form (POST /api/sms/opt-in).
│   │   ├── staffPortal.js    # Staff portal API - composite + account-management endpoints (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-27-staff-portal-redesign-design.md).
│   │   ├── staffShiftActions.js # Staff portal Drop / Cover marketplace endpoints (spec §6.5).
│   │   ├── stripe.js         # Payment intents, payment links, webhooks
│   │   ├── stripeCreateIntent.js # POST /api/stripe/create-intent/:token - extracted from stripe.js (gratuity split) so the gratuity computation doesn't grow the over-cap stripe.js. Mounted by stripe.js via router.use(require('./stripeCreateIntent')).
│   │   ├── stripePayouts.js  # GET/POST /api/stripe-payouts - DB-only payout mirror list/detail + rate-limited sweep trigger (read-side; admin/manager)
│   │   ├── stripeWebhook.js  # Stripe webhook dispatcher, extracted verbatim from stripe.js to keep that file under the line-count cap. Mounted by stripe.js via router.use(require('./stripeWebhook')), so the final path stays /api/stripe/webhook and the raw-body middleware (server/index.js) still applies. The per-event handler BODIES were extracted verbatim into per-concern sibling modules under ./stripeWebhookHandlers/ (behind this thin dispatcher); this file now only does signature verification + routing. Each handler returns via res on an early ack; when it does not send, control falls through to the final `{ received: true }` ack (res.headersSent guards the difference). sendPaymentNotifications lives in utils/stripePaymentNotifications.js.
│   │   ├── telegram.js       # Zul VA-calling OUTBOUND trigger: POST /api/telegram/:secret (secret path + secret_token header + user_id allowlist), NANP validation, confirm-before-dial (YES), claim-then-call bridge
│   │   ├── thumbtack.js      # Thumbtack webhook endpoints (leads, messages, reviews)
│   │   ├── thumbtackAgent.js # Thumbtack box-agent API (/api/admin/thumbtack): email-harvest queue (pending-harvest/email-harvested/harvest-failed/rearm) + auto first-reply queue (pending-first-replies/first-reply-sent/first-reply-failed). Driven by the box-only agent in thumbtack-agent/ (one loop, 10s reply tick, wall-clock ~5-min harvest pass)
│   │   ├── venues.js         # Google Places venue search proxy
│   │   ├── voice.js          # Two-line Twilio Voice webhooks: POST /inbound (forward 0082 → VA_CELL, stamps line=zul), /inbound/primary (forward 1922 → VM_PRIMARY_DIAL_TARGET, stamps line=primary), /bridge (look up target by CallSid → Dial 224→target), /status (failed-leg → Telegram notice), /inbound/missed (shared <Dial> action, per-line greeting + optional press-1 <Gather> + <Record>, interception canary), /inbound/voicemail (recordingStatusCallback: per-line delivery via utils/voicemail). Static audio moved to voiceAssets.js (2026-08-19). /inbound, /inbound/primary, and both voicemail routes fail CLOSED on signature in every environment; the two FORWARD routes run signature BEFORE the limiter (the voicemail webhooks keep their per-CallSid limiter first, deliberately: junk sids collapse to one bucket no real callback lands in)
│   │   ├── voiceAssets.js    # server/routes/voiceAssets.js
│   │   ├── voiceConsultCall.js # Consult call bridge Twilio webhooks (spec 2026-08-25 section 4.3), mounted at /api/voice/consult:
│   │   ├── voiceEscalate.js  # server/routes/voiceEscalate.js
│   │   ├── voiceLeadCall.js  # Lead call bridge Twilio webhooks (spec 2026-07-18 section 4.3), mounted at /api/voice/lead:
│   │   ├── voicemailListen.js # server/routes/voicemailListen.js
│   │   ├── webauthn.js       # Passkey unlock under /api/auth/webauthn (register/assert/credentials; the ONLY 12h-mint site; SENSITIVE-LISTED)
│   │   ├── admin/
│   │   │   ├── applications.js # /applications + /notes + interview scheduling + scorecard + reject/restore/move/reminder
│   │   │   ├── blog.js       # /blog admin endpoints
│   │   │   ├── contractorTipPage.js # /contractors/:userId/tip-page admin actions (patch/rotate-token/generate-stripe/regenerate-stripe/activate/deactivate) + /tips + /tip-feedback review
│   │   │   ├── hiring.js     # Hiring-page-specific endpoints. Lives alongside admin/applications.js but scopes the dashboard's KPI strip + cross-state search separately so the queries can evolve without touching the main applications list endpoint.
│   │   │   ├── inbox.js      # Inbox (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-06-inbox-design.md, section 8). Every route is auth + requireAdminOrManager + asyncHandler, and every person key is checked against spec 8's patterns before anything reads it. The rules, the readers and the text route live in server/utils/inbox/. Personal mail, judged once per mail by the sync (personalMail.js; spec 2026-10-14 decision 6 and section 8), is its owner's alone: the list and the item filter it in the engine, and seen, actions and text refuse it to anyone else with the item's own 404, before they write anything. Those three refuse an e- key with no mail the same way, so they never tell a personal person from an address that never wrote (an e- key is a hash of an address anyone can guess).
│   │   │   ├── index.js      # Composition router for /api/admin/*. Mount order is irrelevant here because each sub-router's paths are non-overlapping (no two sub-routers share a path or a `/:id` catch-all that could shadow another's specific path).
│   │   │   ├── leadCalls.js  # Lead call bridge: needs-attention feed. NARROWED 2026-07-20 per Dallas: a missed or after-hours lead needs no callback (speed-to-lead value dies with the moment; follow-up is the normal email/SMS pipeline), so those are NOT attention items. Only system-fault chains surface: the machine could not place calls at all (Twilio failure, missing config, bad phone data). At healthy steady state this feed is empty and the Sales tab's lead-call items simply never exist. 7-day window; a lead that leaves 'new' clears its item. Driven FROM lead_call_attempts, so pre-feature leads never surface. Read-only; the overview NeedsYouStrip consumes it.
│   │   │   ├── managers.js   # /managers CRUD
│   │   │   ├── nameNotices.js # Informational notice when a staff preferred name is set or changed. Spec §3.5. THIS IS NOT A GATE: the name is live from the moment it is typed, and no name read path consults preferred_name_reviewed_at. The only action is "Got it", which stamps the timestamp. There is deliberately no reject action; the remedy for a bad name is a conversation, and if that conversation ends in a change, it gets made in the profile like any other edit.

Only when nothing is owed does a separate itemized 'Enhancement Lab' invoice carry the remainder. NO PAYMENT UI: no Stripe, no card fields, nothing here takes payment - the money lands on the client's balance paperwork.

This handler is the ONLY writer of stripe_sessions.status = 'processing'; the succeeded and payment_failed handlers release it. Every consumer reads it through utils/paymentInFlight.js.

This script recomputes scheduled_for for PENDING rows only, using the same single-source-of-truth offset map + anchor helper the scheduler now uses.

│   │   └── staffPaymentImport/
│   │       ├── applySeniorityBackfill.js # Apply the HUMAN-APPROVED seniority mapping. Dry-run by default (prints the before→after per row, writes nothing); --apply performs the writes inside a transaction. Idempotent at the ROW level: the UPDATE is guarded with IS DISTINCT FROM, so a second --apply run touches zero rows (it reports them as "already correct") and cannot restamp updated_at, which smsInbound.js uses as a tiebreak for shared inbound numbers. Only include=yes rows with a matched user are written.
│   │       ├── buildReviewSheet.js # Review-sheet builder (spec §7) - walks the payments share, routes each file to its parser, classifies + event-matches outgoing rows, and writes the human review surface plus an Excel-proof manifest and a coverage report. Read-only over the data; writes only into the review dir. No DB.
│   │       ├── ccReports.js  # Loaders for the CheckCherry cross-check reports + the OS known-people export. All tolerate a missing file (return []) - CC reports and known-people.csv are pulled fresh at operation time and may be absent during the offline smoke.
│   │       ├── classify.js   # Row classifier - turns a staging row into a proposed verdict for the review sheet. Order: funding → ignore-patterns → agencies → dictionary hit → unsure.
│   │       ├── config.js     # Staff payment import - pipeline config: boundary date, CLI dir resolution, and the canonical enumeration of every source account (spec 2026-07-10 §2).
│   │       ├── dictionary.js # Payee dictionary + cross-platform name clustering. Seeds people-clusters from the OS known-people export, CC contacts, and CC expense payees, and folds known cross-platform aliases into one cluster. resolve(name) → clusterKey|null.
│   │       ├── eventMatch.js # Opportunistic event attribution (spec §7). Best evidence wins: a. CC expense: same payee-cluster, |amount| exact, |date| ≤ 5 days → booking label b. memo: a date token (M/D) or a " - <suffix>" → memo-derived label c. CC booking proximity: payee in Assigned Staff, payment 0–7 days after Event Date → "<title> (inferred)" d. none → null (acceptable)
│   │       ├── exportKnownPeople.js # One-off, READ-ONLY export of OS people → <review-dir>/known-people.csv. The ONLY pipeline file that touches a DB. Seeds the payee dictionary with OS user ids + onboarding status, and feeds the review sheet's phone-collision check (a stale CC phone must not shadow a real `approved` staffer).
│   │       ├── generateSeniorityMapping.js # READ-ONLY generator: CheckCherry contacts × OS staff → a human-review CSV of proposed hire_date + historical_events_worked. Run exportKnownPeople.js first (it writes <review-dir>/known-people.csv). This script only READS the DB (current hire_date + live event count per matched user); it writes no rows.
│   │       ├── importFromSheet.js # Transactional import of the reviewed sheet → users + profiles + ledger, in ONE BEGIN/COMMIT (spec §6/§7, plan C2). DRY-RUN by DEFAULT: it does the full write inside a transaction, prints the write plan + per-person/per-year totals, then ROLLBACKs. Pass --execute to COMMIT. No email/SMS/activity writes - created users are silent (spec §6); the claim path is the existing forgot-password flow.
│   │       ├── importValidation.js # Sheet validation (spec §4/§6/§7, plan C1). PURE - no DB, no I/O. The import (importFromSheet.js) runs ONLY when validateSheets(...).errors is empty.
│   │       ├── reconcile.js  # Reconciliation report (spec §4, plan C3). READ-ONLY: writes NOTHING to the DB. Post-boundary staff-pay payments (paid_on >= 2026-06-02, no boundary_exception) are NOT in the ledger; this matches each to a PENDING payout so Dallas can mark it paid by hand through the payroll UI (deliberately manual - spec §4).
│   │       ├── staging.js    # Staff payment import - the staging row: one identical shape produced by every parser, plus the money parser and the fingerprint rule (spec §5). Pure, no DB.
│   │       ├── verifyImport.js # Post-import verification (spec §4/§11, plan C3). READ-ONLY. Re-derives the expected facts from the sheet (validateSheets) and asserts the DB agrees. Scoped to THIS sheet's fingerprints - the shared dev DB may hold other lanes' rows, so verification checks the rows this import produced, not the whole table. Exits 1 on ANY mismatch (an incomplete verification is never a pass).

The menu art job owns retries. Under NODE_ENV=test every call rejects unless a test injected a stub (__setTestStub). Only the sanitized descriptor (describeError) may be stored or sent to Sentry, never the SDK message, which can quote the request; prompts and images never.

│       ├── menuArtJob.js     # The menu art job (spec 2026-10-01 sections 5.3, 5.4, 5.5 and 6, Writers; plan Task E4). Every provider call spends real money, so each goes through one gate (drawPiece here; the words step the same way): 1. claim the piece, pending -> working, conditional on its run (a piece Update draft removed, or one re-run, claims nothing); 2. one UPDATE refreshing the active piece's heartbeat and every pending piece of the run (the words step also content.words_heartbeat_at); 3. authorizeCall on that call's own reservation; the call only on true; 4. after the call, every write is conditional on the run again, and the bytes go to R2 only while the run is still current. A lost RUN (the draft run_id moved: Start over superseded it) stops: no further call and no further write. A lost PIECE on a Generate run that is still current (Update draft deleted it mid-run) is skipped and the run goes on; its reservation, never authorized, is released by the settle below. Stored bytes reach a provider only after their key's prefix is re-checked. No pooled client is ever held. Siblings: menuArtJobSteps.js (the piece writes, stored-image reads), menuArtJobWords.js (both words calls) and menuArtJobErrors.js (descriptors, sentences, Sentry, the 429 wait).
│       ├── menuArtJobErrors.js # What the menu art job (menuArtJob.js) records and says when something goes wrong, and its 429 wait (spec 2026-10-01 sections 5.1, 5.5 Retries, Refusals and Errors; plan Task E4). Split from the job to keep each file short; only the job and its siblings call these.
│       ├── menuArtJobSteps.js # The menu art job's piece writes and stored-image reads (menuArtJob.js; spec 2026-10-01 sections 5.4, 5.5 and 6, Writers and R2 keys; plan Task E4). Split from the job to keep each file short; only the job and its siblings call these.
│       ├── menuArtJobWords.js # The words calls of the menu art job (menuArtJob.js; spec 2026-10-01 sections 5.3, 5.5 and 6, Writers; plan Task E4). Split from the job to keep each file short. Both always run on OpenAI.
│       ├── menuArtPrompts.js # The words prompt and schema for the menu art generator (spec 2026-10-01 sections 5.1 and 5.3; plan Task E2). Pure: no database, no provider. The image prompts are menuArtImagePrompts.js, which reuses cut and the limits.
│       ├── menuBackgroundTone.js # The tone of a menu background (spec 2026-10-01 section 5.4, Background tone; section 12; plan Task E3). Pure: sharp only, no database.
│       ├── menuDraftRead.js  # The menu draft read model: the GET /api/proposals/:id/menu-draft payload (spec 2026-10-01 sections 7, 8 and 9; 5.5, Stuck rule). Admin and manager only.
│       ├── menuDraftValidation.js # Server-side validation for the menu draft routes (spec 7.1, 4.5); the client mirrors it. Failures are one ValidationError, fieldErrors keyed by field name. - A PATCH replaces only the top-level content and inputs keys it sends, and server-written keys are refused, never silently ignored. - validatePatch returns only the sections the body sent, so the route writes only those columns: an inputs-only PATCH never writes content back over the job's words fill (the job writes content without bumping version). It says changed: false when every sent value equals the stored one, so the route skips the write and the version bump (an approved draft stays approved). - On a drink only the name, description, Hide, art and the AI flags may change. Every other key is fixed, including any the drafter adds later: it may be sent back only exactly as stored, and a key the stored drink lacks may not be sent at all. - A value sent back unchanged is never refused for its length or count: the server wrote it (catalog descriptions have no cap; Generate or Update draft can leave more than 12 drinks visible), so the limits bind what Dallas changes, and a count over its limit may only go down.
│       ├── menuDrinkSource.js # Turns an event's drink plan into the one shape the menu art generator reads, whichever side feeds it (spec 2026-10-01-menu-art-generator, 5.2, decision 8). - Drinks come from the side the shopping list uses (shopping_list_source), so the menu matches what is poured. The brief (theme, naming, notes, planner image) always comes from `selections`. - Order: signature cocktails (catalog ids), client-typed cocktails, mocktails (catalog ids), client-typed mocktails. A picked id with no catalog row at all is treated as client-typed and joins the end of its group's typed run. - Catalog lookups ignore is_active (drinks are soft-deleted only). - A typed name resolves to a catalog drink through the shopping list's own matcher (exact normalized name, then request_aliases, first wins). A picked drink always beats a typed one, and every drink appears once by key. All pure except loadEventPlan and loadCatalogMaps, which take the db.
│       ├── menuFontPairings.js # The fixed font pairing ids for the menu art generator (spec 10.3).
│       ├── menuImageProvider.js # The one place the menu art job asks for an image (spec 2026-10-01 sections 5.1 and 5.4, decision 11; plan Task E3b). It routes a piece to the wrapper of the provider stamped on it and returns the bytes the job stores:
│       ├── menuKeyOut.js     # Magenta key-out for Gemini drink art (spec 5.4 "Drinks, Gemini", plan Review Focus 6). Gemini cannot return alpha, so the drink prompt asks for a flat #FF00FF background and this turns it into a 1024x1024 PNG with real alpha. Pure: sharp decodes to raw RGBA, plain JS works the pixels, sharp encodes.
│       ├── menuPrintFile.js  # The ONE way a bar menu print file leaves the server. Both downloads stream through here: assigned staff on GET /api/shifts/:shiftId/menu-print (routes/eventDetails.js) and admin/manager on GET /api/proposals/:id/menu-print (routes/proposals/menuPrint.js). Each route keeps its own authorization and key lookup; the traversal guard, the R2 proxy and the caching headers live only here so the two cannot drift apart.
│       ├── menuPrintState.js # The menu print state of an event (spec 2026-10-01 section 8), split from the read model (menuDraftRead.js, which re-exports both). Pure.
│       ├── messageLog.js     # Append-only client-message ledger: pure builders + logClientMessage (fire-and-forget, never throws) + getMessageLogForProposal; written at the sendEmail/sendSMS choke points, read on GET /proposals/:id
│       ├── messageScheduling.js # scheduleMessage(...): idempotent insert of a future touch into the scheduled_messages table
│       ├── messageSuppression.js # shouldSendImmediate(...): shared archive / comm-prefs / bad-contact gate for immediate-send paths
│       ├── metricsQueries.js # Pure metrics query builders + filter parsing. No DB, no side effects - the single source of truth for Dashboard/Financials filtering so the two endpoints cannot drift. Mirrors the pricingEngine.js / bookingWindow.js style.
│       ├── notificationChannelResolver.js # Exports pickChannelsForUserAndCategory, CRITICAL_CATEGORIES, CRITICAL_FALLBACK_ORDER, DEFAULT_CHANNELS. Header generated from the file's exports by migrate-headers on 2026-10-27; replace with what it does.
│       ├── notificationsEnabled.js # Gate for real outbound notifications (Resend email, Twilio SMS, Web Push).
│       ├── onboardingProgress.js # Guarantee an onboarding_progress row exists for a user.
│       ├── onboardingPromotion.js # Onboarding promotion gate (spec 2026-07-22-onboarding-promotion-gate).
│       ├── openaiClient.js   # The ONLY module that requires the openai package (the stripeClient.js and anthropicClient.js precedents; spec 2026-10-01 section 5.1). Menu art asks OpenAI for words and images only through the functions below, so the options pinned here hold for every call.
│       ├── orientationData.js # Assembles the booking/receipt/planner payload for the orientation email
│       ├── outstandingDocuments.js # Which onboarding documents a worker still owes (one predicate, two surfaces)
│       ├── paymentFailedClientNotify.js # Exports notifyClientPaymentFailed. Header generated from the file's exports by migrate-headers on 2026-10-27; replace with what it does.
│       ├── paymentInFlight.js # Bank debit in flight (spec 2026-09-14 section 3.2). ONE definition of "a payment on this proposal is still settling", read by all three checkout rails, the balance reminder ladder, the public invoice and proposal payloads, the polled payment-state route and the admin payment panel. Nothing else reads stripe_sessions.status = 'processing' directly.
│       ├── payrollAccrual.js # Accrue payout records for a completed event. Idempotent: re-running recomputes the system-owned money fields rather than duplicating rows, and never clobbers an admin's edits to hours, rate, late, or adjustments.
│       ├── payrollClawback.js # Auto-clawback for a card tip that was already paid out and is now being refunded (or chargeback funds were withdrawn during a dispute). The bartender's pro-rata share of the clawback amount lands as a NEGATIVE adjustment_cents on a synthetic payout_events row in the bartender's open-period payout, keyed by the ORIGINAL shift so the line labels back.
│       ├── payrollDeferredRetry.js # Re-run placement for tips that deferred while the open pay period was frozen. Idempotent, single-flight, attempt-capped. Fired off the response path after a successful accrual and from the admin Retry button.
│       ├── payrollDisputeNotify.js # Build and send the admin notification when Stripe reinstates funds on a disputed card tip we already paid out. Phase 2 does not auto re-pay (see the "Dispute reinstatement" carve-out at the top of the Phase 2 plan), so this email gives the admin every figure needed to add the positive adjustment on each bartender's next payout manually.
│       ├── payrollEmailTemplates.js # Payroll-domain email templates for Dr. Bartender - admin-facing notifications for the staff payment system (Phase 2+). Carved out of emailTemplates.js to keep that file under the file-size cap. emailTemplates.js re-exports the templates below for backwards compatibility, so consumers that access them by property (emailTemplates.disputeWonAdminNotification) keep working.
│       ├── payrollGuards.js  # cc-import payroll guards.
│       ├── payrollLateTip.js # Roll a card tip that matched a shift in a frozen pay period forward onto each bartender's payout in the open period containing today. The synthetic payout_events row references the ORIGINAL shift so the line still labels back to its true event - the period it lives in is just "where the money lands now."
│       ├── payrollMath.js    # Pure payout math for the staff payment system. No DB, no side effects. All money is integer cents. Mirrors the pricingEngine.js style.
│       ├── payrollPeriods.js # Pure pay-period and payday math for the staff payment system. No DB, no side effects. Mirrors the bookingWindow.js / pricingEngine.js style.
│       ├── payrollProcessing.js # DB helpers shared by the payroll portal routes. Each helper accepts an executor (pool OR a pg client mid-transaction) so callers can either run standalone or join a transaction the route already opened.
│       ├── payrollTips.js    # DB-side tip helpers for payroll: match a tip to the shift (event) it belongs to, and capture the real Stripe processing fee for tips and proposal payments. Fee capture runs at accrual time, not in the tip webhook: when the webhook fires the Stripe charge has usually not settled, so the balance-transaction fee is not yet available.
│       ├── paystubData.js    # server/utils/paystubData.js
│       ├── paystubPdf.js     # server/utils/paystubPdf.js
│       ├── pendingCall.js    # VA-calling DB helpers: upsertPending, claimForDial (conditional UPDATE claim-then-call), attachCallSid, lookupTargetByCallSid, countPlacedSince (daily/per-min cap), recordAudit, pruneVaCallingRows
│       ├── pendingEmailChangeCleanup.js # Daily cleanup of stale pending_email_changes rows (spec §6.10 step 10).
│       ├── phone.js          # Save-time phone validation. Distinct from `sms.js#normalizePhone`, which converts to E.164 for Twilio at send-time. This helper enforces that the stored value is a 10-digit US number - empty strings are coerced to null (so optional fields stay nullable). Strips a leading country code 1 to stay compatible with formatPhone.js (client) which assumes 10-digit storage.
│       ├── positionsNeeded.js # Shape-tolerant parser for shifts.positions_needed. Production holds two historical shapes: a flat string array ["Bartender","Bartender"] and a legacy object array [{position:'bartender',count:2}]. Every reader of positions_needed must go through this, never a bare JSON.parse, or legacy object-shaped rows render as garbage. Malformed input normalizes to [].
│       ├── potionCatalog.js  # Potion catalog: derives every legacy shopping-list constant (PARS_100, SPIRIT_PARS, beer/wine style maps, BASIC_MIXERS, GARNISHES, ALWAYS_INCLUDE, SPIRIT_MIXER_PAIRINGS, INGREDIENT_MAP) as a slice of the ONE par_items catalog, and resolves recipe/free-text ingredient names to purchasable items through per-row aliases.
