Header generated from the file's exports by migrate-headers on 2026-10-27; replace with what it does.
│       ├── preEventScheduling.js # Exports computeScheduledFor, shouldScheduleLongLeadRecap, schedulePreEventReminders, SEND_HOUR_LOCAL. Header generated from the file's exports by migrate-headers on 2026-10-27; replace with what it does.
│       ├── presence.js       # Pure presence helpers: derivation, predicates, and time bucketing. No DB calls (mirror pricingEngine.js) so every rule is unit-testable. Spec: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-02-presence-tracker-design.md
│       ├── presenceActivity.js # In-memory sign-of-life map + throttled DB flush (spec: Sign of life). The map is exact per-request truth within this process; the DB column is the durable shadow (max 60s stale), for cross-instance visibility. The sweep reads GREATEST(map, DB). Fire-and-forget: nothing here may ever block or fail a request.
│       ├── presenceNotify.js # Dibs-edge notifier (spec 2026-07-06-presence-dibs-design.md): when a mutation BY the fallback owner moves the lead pointer, ping the user it moved off/onto via their nudge channel. Fire-and-forget: never rejects, never blocks the mutation. Gated skips are silent; only genuine send failures warn + Sentry; confirmed sends log one audit line.
│       ├── presenceScheduler.js # Stale-desk nudge + auto-flip sweep, every 15 min (spec: Nudge and auto-flip scheduler). nudged_at stamps ONLY on a confirmed send; an ignored nudge flips the user to away with the interval closed AT the nudge time.
│       ├── presenceStore.js  # DB layer for the presence tracker. Every multi-statement write is a transaction; the one-open-interval invariant is enforced by the partial unique index (INSERT side) and by id-scoped guarded UPDATEs (close side).
│       ├── pricingEngine.js  # Pricing Engine - pure functions, zero DB dependencies. Takes package/addon data as arguments, returns a pricing snapshot.
│       ├── pricingSnapshot.js # Pricing snapshot version stamp + tolerant reader.
│       ├── proposalExtrasFold.js # Fold client-driven extras (add-ons / syrups / bars) into a proposal's contract: recompute the pricing snapshot, move any negotiated override by the CATALOG delta of what changed, persist total_price + snapshot, and re-evaluate payment status. Extracted VERBATIM from the drink-plan submit financial path (2026-07-20, pp2-lab-fold) so the Enhancement Lab folds additions through the exact same battle-tested sequence - one money path, two callers. Callers own: proposal_addons upserts/deletes BEFORE calling, the before/after legs, invoice refresh AFTER (refreshUnlockedInvoices + createAdditionalInvoiceIfNeeded), and their own activity-log entries.
│       ├── proposalGroupCommit.js # Choice-commit for proposal option groups. Runs INSIDE the settle transaction of the winning option's first payment (webhook or admin record-payment), BEFORE the amount_paid/invoice-link step, on the caller's dbClient. It does NOT create the winner's invoice (the caller does that AFTER stamping payment_type, so Deposit vs Full is picked correctly) and does NOT run the best-effort marketing/change-request reaps (those run post-commit in the caller, matching today's ->archived semantics so a loser's reap failure can never roll back a paid winner).
│       ├── proposalGroups.js # Proposal option groups: the admin-side model for "compare your options".
│       ├── proposalInsert.js # Shared proposal-row builder. ONE source of the proposals INSERT shape + venue composition + addon insert, so the manual create route and the Thumbtack auto-draft util can never drift. Pricing, status transitions, invoices, and emails are the CALLER's job - this only writes the row(s).

│       ├── proposalRules.js  # Server twin of client/src/utils/proposalRules.js. CJS. The constants below MUST stay in sync with client/src/pages/website/quoteWizard/bundleConfig.js (same manual-twin discipline as eventTypes.js). This twin now mirrors the FULL bundle config - BYOB_BUNDLE_SLUGS, MIXER_SLUGS, BUNDLE_INCLUDED, BUNDLE_UNAVAILABLE (and the derived BUNDLE_COVERED) - not just the two slug arrays, so the server can strip bundle-covered add-ons before pricing. validateProposalRules is the AUTHORITATIVE gate - a stale tab or scripted POST bypasses the client, so every rule the wizard UI enforces is re-checked here.

The shopping-list generator translates demand into purchasable units and applies the per-role buffer multipliers (settings keys shopping_buffer_*); this module only models WHO drinks HOW MUCH of WHAT.

The headers match that helper's. The body is buffered rather than streamed, because a thumbnail is resized in memory and these layers are bounded (a 2560x3200 JPEG background is the largest).

An empty requested list is treated as "any role" (legacy rows backfilled from a single position, or pre-migration rows with no ranking).

Information only. Nothing here ranks, sorts or scores; autoAssign.js owns ranking and is not a caller.

│       ├── staleProposalSweep.js # staleProposalSweep - archive past-dated proposals that were never booked.
│       ├── storage.js        # Cloudflare R2 upload + signed URL helpers + readFile (an object's bytes and content type, time and size bounded; the menu art job and r2Proxy.js)
│       ├── stripeClient.js   # Central Stripe client factory with test-mode toggle.
│       ├── stripePaymentNotifications.js # Stripe payment-notification emails + SMS (lifted out of the webhook handler so server/routes/stripe.js and server/routes/stripeWebhook.js stay under the size cap). Pure post-commit notifier: takes (proposalId, amountCents, paymentType), reads the proposal/client, and sends the client receipt/orientation + the admin notification. Self-contained - depends only on its args and these module-level helpers.
│       ├── stripePayoutSync.js # Stripe payout tracking - read-side mirror sync (spec 2026-07-01). All ingest paths converge here through idempotent upserts keyed on Stripe ids. OWNERSHIP RULE: only syncPayout ever sets/changes payout_id on a line; the pending path is insert-only (ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING), or it would un-claim settled lines and flip them back to "in transit".
│       ├── stripeRouteHelpers.js # Stripe route helpers extracted from server/routes/stripe.js so the create-intent sub-router (server/routes/stripeCreateIntent.js) can share them without growing the over-cap stripe.js. Pure move - no behavior change.
│       ├── telegram.js       # Telegram Bot API wrapper (VA calling): sendTelegramMessage/setTelegramWebhook/getTelegramWebhookInfo (raw fetch, no dep), verifyTelegramSecret (constant-time), isNewUpdate (update_id de-dupe), sendTelegramAudio (multipart voicemail mp3 upload, 120s timeout vs 8s for messages)
│       ├── thumbtackProposalDraft.js # Thumbtack auto-draft builder (createDraftProposalFromLead) + pure field mappers (event-type keyword map, ET date/time split, admin-notes block)
│       ├── tipHandleValidation.js # Validates and normalizes contractor tip-page handles before they're persisted or rendered into the public tip page. paypal_url in particular flows into an <a href> on TipPage.jsx - accepting any URL would let a manager-or-admin set a phishing target dressed up as a paypal.me link.
│       ├── tipMethods.js     # Shared tip-method derivation for /api/me/tip-page + /api/public/tip/:token: read-side handle re-validation (drops unnormalizable stored values, Sentry-warns), availability, then saved-order resolution. One derivation so a downloadable printed sign can't advertise a method the post-scan chooser page refuses to render
│       ├── tipPageLifecycle.js # server/utils/tipPageLifecycle.js
│       ├── tipPaymentLinks.js # server/utils/tipPaymentLinks.js
│       ├── tokens.js         # Canonical public-token shape validation. proposals/invoices/drink_plans tokens, users.calendar_token, payment_profiles.tip_page_token, and sms_messages.group_id are all Postgres UUID columns, so a non-UUID :param casts-and-throws (22P02) and surfaces as a 500 + Sentry noise instead of a clean 404. Validate the shape up front, before any DB query.
│       ├── twilioSignature.js # Shared isValidTwilioRequest (Twilio webhook signature check); policy on failure stays per-router (fail-closed everywhere except voice.js's /bridge and /status and sms.js's /inbound and /status, which keep the dev warn-and-allow)
│       ├── urls.js           # URL helpers - keep client-facing links on the public domain, admin links on the admin domain, and backend-hosted links (unsubscribe, webhooks) on the API origin so a single misconfiguration can't route customers into /admin/* or pin an unsubscribe link to a static SPA that can't handle the request.
│       ├── usPhone.js        # US/NANP phone validation: toUsE164, isUsE164 (normalizePhone + strict +1 NANP gate, rejects intl + 900/976) - primary VA-calling toll-fraud control
│       ├── vaCallingScheduler.js # server/utils/vaCallingScheduler.js
│       ├── venueAddress.js   # Structured venue address: compose + validate. Pure, no DB calls. event_location and shifts.location are derived from these fields. VENUE_STATES + composeVenueLocation are mirrored client-side in client/src/components/VenueAddressFields.js (VENUE_STATES + formatVenue) - kept in sync manually (same pattern as eventTypes.js). Edit both together.
│       ├── voicemail.js      # server/utils/voicemail.js
│       ├── voicemailEscalation.js # server/utils/voicemailEscalation.js
│       ├── voicemailLine.js  # server/utils/voicemailLine.js
│       ├── voicemailTwiml.js # server/utils/voicemailTwiml.js
│       ├── webhookEventsPruneScheduler.js # Hourly prune of `webhook_events` to a 30-day window (gated by RUN_WEBHOOK_EVENTS_PRUNE_SCHEDULER)
│       ├── xmlEscape.js      # Escape XML metacharacters (& < >) for safe interpolation into TwiML element text. Extracted from the inline copy in server/routes/sms.js (the inbound-SMS <Message> handler) so the SMS route and the new voice TwiML routes share one implementation. Order matters: & is escaped first.
│       ├── __fixtures__/
│       │   └── gemini-drink-raw.jpg
│       ├── comms/
│       │   ├── registry.js   # Comms action registry (spec 4.1). Auto-discovers every module in ./actions at require time so downstream lanes ADD an action file without editing this one (the lane model forbids cross-lane edits to shared files).
│       │   ├── render.js     # Renders editable "parts" into a branded email. Parts are the compose-modal contract (spec 4.1): the admin edits `subject` and `bodyText` (plain prose); `heading` and `cta` are fixed by the action so an edit can never break the token link that makes the email work. bodyText is HTML-escaped here, so edited prose cannot inject markup.
│       │   └── actions/
│       │       ├── consultRecap.js # Comms action: consult_recap (spec 4.4). Converts the post-consult client recap email (the second stale-snapshot sender) into the compose-first contract AND fixes its recipient bug.
│       │       ├── drinkPlanNudge.js # Comms action: drink_plan_nudge (spec 4.4). Ports the manual "Resend planner link" send (POST /api/drink-plans/:id/resend-nudge) into the compose-first contract. This is the ADMIN manual resend, not the scheduled T-21 nudge, so it deliberately does NOT suppress on an already-filled plan.
│       │       ├── drinkPlanNudgeReenroll.js # Comms action: drink_plan_nudge_reenroll (plan P1). Ports POST /admin/proposals/:id/reenroll-drink-plan-nudge (server/routes/admin/ccImport/proposalActions.js). ensureSideEffects performs the re-enroll: clear the durable nudge suppression (cc-transfer sets it so Dallas can intro-note the client first) and (re)schedule the T-21 email+SMS nudges - idempotent, since scheduleDrinkPlanNudge no-ops on a pending duplicate and setting nudge_suppressed=false twice changes nothing. dispatch is the NEW compose-modal capability: an immediate drink-plan nudge on the selected channels (the legacy route only scheduled - see the deprecated route note, which preserves schedule-only behavior and does not auto-dispatch).
│       │       ├── invoiceSend.js # Comms action: invoice_send (spec 4.4). NEW capability - there was no existing "send this invoice to the client" seam; the UI wave calls POST /api/comms directly (no legacy route to rewire).
│       │       ├── paymentReminder.js # Comms action: payment_reminder (plan P1). Ports POST /api/proposals/:id/send-reminder (server/routes/proposals/actions.js:90). It is a BALANCE reminder - named honestly so the modal copy never implies a receipt. MONEY RULE: the outstanding balance is derived exactly as the legacy route (total_price - amount_paid) and rendered by the SAME formula the parts template uses; no amount is invented or recomputed differently, and no money column is written. The legacy route has no state side effect, so ensureSideEffects only enforces the "balance must be positive" guard.
│       │       ├── portalInvite.js # Comms action: portal_invite (plan P1). Ports POST /api/proposals/:id/portal-invite (server/routes/proposals/lifecycle.js). The portal sits behind OTP login (email a one-time code), so NO token rides in the link and nothing is minted - the invite has no state side effect (ensureSideEffects is a no-op). Email is the default channel per the prefer-email default; SMS is available when a phone exists but off by default.
│       │       ├── proposalResend.js # Comms action: proposal_resend (plan P1). Ports POST /api/proposals/:id/resend (server/routes/proposals/lifecycle.js) into the compose-first contract. The legacy resend re-sends the proposalSent email + initial-proposal SMS, leaving status and sent timestamps untouched - so this action has NO state side effect (ensureSideEffects is a validate-only no-op). dispatch OWNS its ledger writes with skipLog so a provider throw can never leave a sent-but-unlogged message (the 7/16 Brandon Martin failure class).
│       │       ├── proposalSend.js # Comms action: proposal_send (plan P2, spec 4.4). The compose-first INITIAL send: the creation flow now saves the proposal as a draft first, then this action's ensureSideEffects performs the legacy PATCH /:id/status 'sent' transition (row-locked flip + sent_at + activity log + invoice, one transaction, mirroring server/routes/proposals/lifecycle.js) so a proposal is never committed 'sent' without its invoice. Cancel in the modal leaves a plain draft (existing draft semantics + cleanup scheduler).
│       │       ├── proposalSendGroup.js # Comms action: proposal_send_group (plan P1). Ports POST /api/proposals/:id/send-group (server/routes/proposals/groups.js -> utils/groupSend.js, since deleted 2026-08-14 - this action is the only implementation now). EMAIL ONLY: a comparison goes out as one proposalOptionsSent "compare your options" email; there is deliberately no per-option text message. ensureSideEffects runs the exact groupSend transaction (FOR UPDATE lock - the AB-BA-safe ordering - transition every draft member to 'sent' with NO per-option invoice and NO per-option proposalSent comms), idempotently: a second call finds no newly-draft members and applies nothing. dispatch OWNS the single compare email + its ledger row.
│       │       └── shoppingListApprove.js # Comms action: shopping_list_approve (spec 4.4). Ports the PATCH /:id/shopping-list/approve behavior into the compose-first contract: side effects (the atomic pending_review -> approved flip, now also writing the client-facing approved snapshot per spec 4.9) run in ensureSideEffects, idempotently; dispatch sends the possibly-edited message and OWNS its ledger writes (sendEmail is called with skipLog so a Resend SDK throw can never produce a sent-but-unlogged email again - the 7/16 Brandon Martin failure).
│       └── inbox/
│           ├── actions.js    # The taps (spec 4.4 and 8): On it, Let go, Done, Snooze, Wake, Reopen, Undo, and the deliberate open. inbox_actions is append-only: Undo stamps undone_at and nothing is deleted. Every write clears the engine cache, so the next list, item or badge read already shows it.
│           ├── aiRead.js     # The AI read (spec 2026-10-06 section 6): two fixed system prompts, two output schemas, and the two calls. No I/O of its own: the caller passes the client (anthropicClient.js builds the real one, tests pass a stub) and stores what comes back.
│           ├── aiRedact.js   # What a message may still say once it leaves the OS for the AI read (spec 2026-10-06 section 6.2). Pure: no I/O.
│           ├── aiStatus.js   # The AI read's status for the feed line (spec 4.6: off, paused at its daily cap, failing at 3 or more error reads in the last hour, else on) and the daily-cap count the read job gates on. One definition with two readers, the read job (whether to read) and the inbox engine's ai.status (what the feed line says), so the line can never say "on" while the job is paused.
│           ├── aliases.js    # Moved person keys (spec 5.2, amendment 22), PURE. A key names a person as the index resolved them when the tap was made, and that can change: an unknown number becomes a client, a Thumbtack lead gains one, a staffer leaves the send-eligible set. Before the rules run, every action and seen mark stored under a key that moved is folded onto the person's current key, in this order (database review, 2026-10-08); rules 2 and 4 ask the people index the way the item route's resolveKey (engine.js) does: 1. a key that still has events stays exactly where it is, so a live item keeps its own Done, Snooze and Claim; 2. a p- key asks the live people index, at the tap's time, and moves when the index names someone else (an unknown number became a client); 3. only then an event alias: the p- key of a phone whose SMS events all name ONE person (a phone on two people's texts never aliases); 4. an s- key of a staffer who is no longer send-eligible follows their profile phone to the key the index names, when that key has events; 5. a t- key follows its lead to the client the lead now belongs to; 6. an e- key asks the index's keyByEmailHash (no time in it) and moves when its address now names a client or staffer (spec 2026-10-14 5.2). A personal mail's key (people.personalEmailKey) is never in that map, so it never moves, whoever its sender becomes; 7. a z- key follows its Zola lead to the client the lead now belongs to. Reads need nothing here: the rules match a read by subject_ref alone, whatever person_key it was written under.
│           ├── cache.js      # The engine's 30-second in-process cache (spec 8), shared by the list, the item and the badge. Every write clears it: seen, actions, Undo, an Inbox text, the Messages reply and the Twilio status callback. A computation that was in flight when a write landed still answers the callers who asked before the write, but is never stored.
│           ├── classify.js   # What counts, for the Inbox rules (spec 2026-10-06, sections 5.3 and 5.4, and spec 2026-10-14 for email and Zola). PURE: every judgment reads facts the readers copied off the source rows, so each one is a unit test over invented fixtures.
│           ├── constants.js  # Inbox constants (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-06-inbox-design.md, sections 4, 5.7 and 8). Code, not env vars: moving a window changes what the rules decide, so it goes through review like any other rules change.
│           ├── context.js    # The context card (spec 4.2), loaded per opened item. Shapes live in contextFormat.js (pure); this file only reads. The staff card uses the canonical predicates: shiftNotFinishedSql for "upcoming" and openSlotsSql for the roster fill (settings.js:133-150 records what drift costs).
│           ├── contextFormat.js # Context-card shapes (spec 4.2), PURE. Field names are pinned in the plan for lane inbox-page. Money is in DOLLARS, the unit proposals stores.
│           ├── details.js    # Pass 2 (spec 5.7): full rows, by ref, for the people the rules keep. Calls and proposal sends carry everything in their header already. A gm event is looked up by its readRef ('gm:<id>'), which every suffixed sibling of one Sent mail shares, and loaded by its row id (spec 2026-10-14 section 5.1).
│           ├── engine.js     # The Inbox engine (spec 5.7 as amended by 18, and spec 8). Two passes behind one 30-second in-process cache that the list, the item, the badge and the AI read job share: pass 1: light headers (no message text) for every row since INBOX_HISTORY_START, and the rules over them, which say who is waiting, snoozed, or closed in the last 7 days; pass 2: full rows (bodies, media, subjects) for those people only. Pass 1 is a header scan, not SQL aggregates, so its cost grows with all the traffic since the floor: a few thousand rows today. Past HEADER_WARN_ROWS (every pass-1 row: the seven readers' headers, the AI reads and the taps) it warns once per process, in the log and in Sentry, the signal to move pass 1 to aggregates. A source query that fails fails the whole load: a partial list that looks complete is the one thing Inbox must never show (spec 12).
│           ├── feeds.js      # Feed health (spec 4.6): when each source last delivered anything, by created_at. Inbound only for the lines; a row with no metadata.to, or a To that is not a registry line, is the 888 (spec 9). A source quiet for 48 hours or more gets the dot. Information, not an alarm: the 224 lines are quiet by nature. Gmail and Zola (spec 2026-10-14 section 4.6): Gmail is the time of the last GOOD sync, not the last mail (a quiet mailbox is healthy, a stuck sync is not), with a status: off with no key or with the sync switched off (the aiStatus.js rule), failing after GMAIL_FAILING_TICKS failed ticks in a row, catching_up while the backfill pages, else on. Zola is the time of its last stored notice of any kind, quiet by nature: every Zola row, and only a Zola row, carries its lead (the sync links one in the insert's transaction), so the lead column finds them through idx_gmail_messages_zola_at.
│           ├── firstText.js  # The first-text prefix (spec 4.3): the first text EVER sent from a 224 line to a number starts with "Dr. Bartender: ", so the person knows who the new number is. The server adds it and the reply box shows it before sending. Shared by the Inbox text route and the Messages reply, which lane sms-lines lets default to the sender's own 224 line. A failed send never counts as the first one: the person never saw it. Neither does one whose failure the status callback reported before its row existed (sms_status_orphans, lane sms-lines) and that no fold has marked failed yet.
│           ├── gmailRules.js # Which mail counts, decided at sync time (spec 2026-10-14 sections 5.2 and 5.5), PURE: no I/O. gmailSync.js fetches a message, reads it here, applies classifyMessage and stores what it says. Nothing here logs: a header value is someone's mail.
│           ├── gmailSync.js  # The Gmail sync tick (spec 2026-10-14 section 5.5), registered in server/index.js as the 60-second gmail_sync scheduler. Each tick: 1. with GMAIL_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY unset, does nothing (createGmailClient returns null); 2. inserts the single gmail_sync_state row if it is missing, then claims its lease with one statement; no row back means another instance (a deploy runs two side by side) holds it, and the tick ends; 3. first sync: reads the mailbox's history id BEFORE the first page, then lists mail since the history floor in two passes, Sent first and then everything else, page by page, each page oldest first. Sent goes first because the listing runs newest first: a reply or a bounce must find its Sent row already stored, or rule 9 and the bounce rule miss it for good. Steady state: history.list from the stored id. Lost history (Google keeps about a week): the same listing from last_ok_at minus a day, its date fixed in the cursor so it holds while last_ok_at moves; 4. reads each message, applies the counting rules (gmailRules.js) and stores what counts, one transaction per message, every side effect gated on the insert returning a row. The personal judgment (personalMail.js) runs in that transaction, before the insert, and is stored on the row: it is made once, here, and never again; 5. stops at GMAIL_SYNC_MAX_PER_TICK messages or GMAIL_SYNC_TICK_MS.
│           ├── lastHumanRows.js # The rows the one "last human-involved line" rule reads for a client, over their WHOLE history (decision 9 has no date window; spec section 17 item 20 asks for one rule). The Messages reply (routes/sms.js) and the Inbox reply area (reply.js) both read them, so a client whose newest human-involved text predates the Inbox history floor keeps the same line on both surfaces. The WHERE clause mirrors lastHumanLineFromRows (smsLines.js: their texts, relay texts included, and our human replies that did not fail) only so the newest candidate is the one row fetched; that function still decides.
│           ├── mailText.js   # The person's own words out of one Gmail message (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-inbox-email-zola-design.md, section 5.7). Pure: no I/O. The sync stores what this returns and nothing more: never the raw mail, never an attachment, never the quoted history (decision 16).
│           ├── normalize.js  # One event per real-world send, plus the cross-row matches the rules need (spec 2026-10-06, sections 5.1, 5.3 and 5.4). PURE. Runs once over every reader's rows, before the rules: 1. drop anything before the history floor (decision 18); 2. collapse a comparison's group_sent rows into one send; 3. link each message_log SMS row to its sms_messages twin (same Twilio SID). The twin takes the ledger row's failure, sent_by and message type, so a "Send to client" or event-details SMS (sender_id NULL by design, lane send-attribution) keeps its human author. A send that threw has no SID on either side: its failed text and its failed ledger row pair when they are one person's within 10 seconds (amendment 23); 4. a proposal send absorbs its message_log deliveries within 5 minutes, either side; a lone proposal-send ledger row with no activity row (the send_now create path) stays as a send of its own; 5. absorbed and twinned ledger rows disappear: the sms: and pal: refs win; 6. a Thumbtack relay notice with no Customer message within 15 minutes becomes a "never received" item, and a matched one is dropped; an unmatched one that quotes their words (Task 18 F1) is their Thumbtack message instead, which the AI may read; 7. the Thumbtack auto first reply is marked (within 2 minutes); 8. an ambiguous opt word that also opted the person out gets an "Opted out of texts" companion line. Output events carry meta.normalized; already-normalized input passes straight through, so the rules may call this on either. The pass-through is all or nothing: a list that mixes normalized and raw events re-runs every step on all of them (a proposal send loses its deliveries, opt-out companions double). Normalize a list once, and never hand it a mix of the two.
│           ├── payload.js    # HTTP bodies (snake_case) for GET /api/admin/inbox and GET /api/admin/inbox/:personKey (contract). PURE: the engine hands in the rules result, the directory of names and kinds, and whatever it loaded.
│           ├── people.js     # The people index (spec 5.2): who a phone number is, decided at READ time, so a text recorded before its sender became a client joins that client's item. Loaded once per engine snapshot; every resolver here is pure over it. 1. our own numbers never form an item: the three lines, ADMIN_PHONE, VA_CELL, and every admin's and manager's contractor phone and presence nudge phone (Dallas's staff account shares the 312, so ADMIN_PHONE covers it); 2. clients by the last 10 digits, newest first (lookupSender's order); 3. the send-eligible staff set, the one the text route can reach (role staff or manager, onboarding submitted/reviewed/approved, an agreement on file), newest profile first. Applicants are not staff here; 4. a post-rollout Thumbtack proxy number, as the newest lead created before the message (findThumbtackProxyLead's rule, narrowed by time); 5. anything else is p-<last 10 digits>. Email addresses (spec 2026-10-14 section 5.2), compared trimmed and lowercased, resolve the same way: 1. our own addresses never form an item: the mailbox, the personal alias, the address FROM_EMAIL sends from, ADMIN_EMAIL, the INBOX_OWN_EMAILS list, every admin's and manager's users.email, and Zola's vendor address. Not the whole domain: a staffer's mailbox there is staff; 2. clients by clients.email, newest client first (the unique index is case-sensitive, so two spellings of one address can coexist); 3. the send-eligible staff set by users.email, newest profile first; 4. anyone else is e-<the first 16 hex of the SHA-256 of the address>, so the key never carries the address. A Zola couple is its lead's client when linked, else z-<zola_leads.id>.
│           ├── personKey.js  # Person keys (spec 5.2): one Inbox item per person. A key is an IDENTITY, never an address. Replies go to the number the person last texted from (decision 29), so nothing ever rebuilds a phone number from a key. c-<clients.id> a client s-<users.id> a send-eligible staffer p-<last 10 digits> a number that is neither t-<negotiation_id> a Thumbtack thread whose lead has no client (a string) e-<16 hex> an email address that is neither: the first 16 hex of the SHA-256 of the lowercased address, so the key carries no address (spec 2026-10-14 section 5.2) z-<zola_leads.id> a Zola couple whose lead has no client
│           ├── personalMail.js # Personal mail (spec 2026-10-14 decision 6, as the owner decided it on 2026-10-15): each mail is judged ON ITS OWN, ONCE, by the sync, when it is stored, and the answer is stored on the row (gmail_messages.personal and personal_to). Nothing later changes it: a sender who becomes a client, a staffer or a marketing lead, or who writes to the business mailbox, never moves a stored personal mail to business, and never a business mail to personal. gmailSync.storeMessage calls judgePersonal inside the message's own transaction, through its connection. inbound (not a Zola notice, not a bounce) is personal when its sender was nobody we knew when it arrived (no client, staffer, operator or marketing lead whose record existed at the mail's `at`) and its To and Cc name none of the business's addresses: either they hold the owner alias alone among ours, or they name none of ours at all (we were Bcc'd, a list, a forward), since an unclear shape stays private. The business addresses are index.businessEmails: the mailbox, the no-reply address FROM_EMAIL sends from, ADMIN_EMAIL, the INBOX_OWN_EMAILS list and every admin's and manager's own address but the owner's (an operator in copy is business: the sender copied our team; the owner's own account is not the team), never the alias. Sent ANSWERING decides first, whichever of our addresses it went from. A Sent mail that answers an inbound (In-Reply-To, else the newest inbound in its Gmail thread before it, the readers' rule; never a forward) takes that inbound's side: it answers a personal inbound, and it is personal for that inbound's sender and for each recipient nobody knew when it was sent; it answers a business inbound, and it is business, so the business item gets its reply and can close. Only a mail that answers nothing (a fresh send, or a forward) reads its From: from the owner alias it is personal for each recipient nobody knew; from anywhere else it is business. A client or staffer copied on it keeps the business event either way. personal_to lists the recipients it is personal for; personal is true when that list holds anyone. Addresses compare trimmed and lowercased, as people.resolveEmail does; a +tag is cut only when asking whether an address is one of ours. One lookup (SQL.knownAt) answers who was known, and only for a mail that could be personal. A database error rejects the judgment, so the store fails with it (gmailSync's isDbOutage decides whose fault): a mail whose judgment could not run is never stored as business.
│           ├── readCalls.js  # Reader: connected bridge calls (spec 5.1 and decision 21), the lead call bridge and the consult call bridge. Shorter calls still show in the thread; the 60-second rule and the consult no-answer rule live in classify.js.
│           ├── readGmail.js  # Reader: gmail_messages, the email rows (spec 2026-10-14 section 5.1). The sync (gmailSync.js) decided at storage which mail counts; this decides WHO each row is about, at read time, through the people index (spec 5.2), so mail stored before its sender became a client joins that client's item. Pass 1 reads headers (no subject, no words); pass 2 (loadGmailDetails) loads the subject and the words for the people the rules keep, by row id. inbound the From address names the person; our own addresses (and so the mail the sync never stores) form nothing. outbound a Sent mail answers, in order, the person of the stored inbound it replies to (In-Reply-To names it, else the newest inbound in its Gmail thread), then every recipient in To, Cc and Bcc that resolves to someone not us, so a reply sent to a Reply-To address still answers the person who wrote. A forward (its subject starts Fwd: or Fw:) answers its recipients only: Gmail keeps it in the thread it forwards, and passing a client's question to a bartender answers the bartender, not the client. One event per person: gm:<id> alone, or gm:<id>:1, gm:<id>:2 ..., all sharing meta.readRef = 'gm:<id>' so the mail is read once (spec 6). A mail that reached two or more staffers answers none of them (spec 5.4, the group-text rule): meta.staffGroup on those. A bounce fails the events of the people it reached through the addresses it names (bounced_to), or every event of the mail when it names none. system an auto-reply, either direction (meta.sent on our own), and a bounce. A bounce is about the people of the Sent mail it names (In-Reply-To, else the newest Sent mail in its thread) that its failed addresses reached, never its machine sender. A recipient at Zola's relay domain is never a person here: readZola.js ties a reply sent there to its couple.
│           ├── readJob.js    # The AI read job (spec 2026-10-06 section 6.4), registered in server/index.js as the 60-second inbox_ai_read scheduler. Each tick: 1. with ANTHROPIC_API_KEY unset, does nothing; 2. turns any read left pending over 5 minutes into an error (a crash after reserving), so it is retried like any other error; 3. pauses when the rolling 24-hour attempt count reaches INBOX_AI_DAILY_CAP; 4. takes the engine's subjects from the badge's cached snapshot, up to BADGE_MAX_AGE_MS (2 minutes) old, keeps the readable ones with no ok or refused read and fewer than 3 attempts (checked against inbox_reads itself, since the snapshot can predate the last tick's writes), oldest first, at most 20; 5. reserves each as pending BEFORE the call, so a store that fails after a good call can never repeat that call every tick, then reads and stores.
│           ├── readMessageLog.js # Reader: message_log (spec 5.1 and 5.4), with the bounce join: a Resend email.bounced event keyed by the row's provider_id makes the send failed. Rows with no client are skipped (5.2). There is no body column; an email shows its subject, an SMS row its 140-character preview.
│           ├── readProposalSends.js # Reader: proposal sends from proposal_activity_log (spec 5.4): status_changed or status_force_changed to 'sent', 'resent', and 'group_sent'. Whether one counts (an admin or manager sent it, and a delivery went out) is decided by the rules after normalize.js absorbs its message_log deliveries. The website wizard never writes these rows, so a site-born proposal is never a reply.
│           ├── readSms.js    # Reader: sms_messages (spec 5.1 and 5.2). Pass 1 reads light headers for every row since the history floor and ships no body longer than 12 characters, which is enough to classify a bare keyword or a CONFIRM / CANT. Pass 2 (loadSmsDetails) loads bodies, media and failure reasons for the people the rules keep (5.7).
│           ├── readThumbtack.js # Reader: thumbtack_messages (spec 5.1). Both sides land here: Customer rows are them writing, Business rows are a reply whose author the OS cannot know. A message reaches a person only through thumbtack_leads.negotiation_id.
│           ├── readZola.js   # Reader: gmail_messages, the Zola rows (spec 2026-10-14 section 5.1). Every zola_ row carries its lead (the sync links one, even to an unparsed notice), and the lead is the person: its client when linked, else z-<lead id>. inquiry, message their words, inbound, kind message; unparsed inbound, kind zola_unread, which reads ZOLA_UNREAD_TEXT, counts, and the AI may never close (the tt_missed rule); closed a system line, kind zola_closed: the couple closed their inquiry, a close by rule (decision 19). A Gmail reply to a message notice reaches the couple through Zola's relay, so a Sent mail is their reply when its In-Reply-To names a stored message notice (never for a forward, which answers its recipients only), or when it went to Zola's relay domain and its thread holds one. The join runs HERE, at read time, never at sync time: the backfill lists newest first, so the reply is often stored before the notice it answers. The reply's ref is gm:<id>:z, its readRef gm:<id>, so a Sent mail that also answers an email person (readGmail.js, gm:<id> or gm:<id>:<n>) never shares a ref with it.
│           ├── reasons.js    # Recently handled reasons (spec 4.5), PURE. "You" means the viewer.
│           ├── reply.js      # The reply area of an opened item and the target of an Inbox text (spec 4.3, 5.8, 8 and decision 29). One resolver serves both, so the box the admin sees and the route that sends can never disagree about who, which number, or which lines.
│           ├── replyRules.js # Who a reply goes to, and the rows the line rule reads (decision 29, spec 5.8). PURE, over the person's normalized events.
│           ├── rules.js      # The Inbox rules (spec 2026-10-06, sections 5.5 and 5.6). PURE: no I/O and no clock read. computeInbox takes every event the readers produced plus the stored taps, opens and AI reads, and says for each person whether they are waiting, snoozed, recently handled, or quiet, and what the AI should read. Every case in spec section 14 "Rules" is a test in rules.*.test.js.
│           ├── rules.testkit.js # Invented fixtures for the pure Inbox suites (normalize, rules.*, subjects, thread, payload). Not a test file: no .test.js suffix, so node --test never runs it on its own. Every name, number and message here is made up (the repo is public), and phone numbers sit in the 555 range.
│           ├── subjects.js   # What the AI reads (spec 6.1 and 6.2), PURE. At most one inbound and one outbound subject per person, each with the slice lane inbox-ai's job redacts and sends. Shape agreed with that lane: every slice line carries its event ref, and context=true marks the up to three lines before the anchor.
│           ├── textSend.js   # The Inbox text (spec 8; spec amendments 2, 3 and 8), behind POST /api/admin/inbox/:personKey/text. The order is the safety: 1. check the request itself (400; nothing is reserved); 2. reserve the send_id in inbox_sends before anything else: a repeat gets the stored answer and sends nothing, and a repeat while the first is still running gets 409; 3. from a fresh read, resolve who and which line (422), the opt-out (409) and a bad number (422), then add the first-text prefix; 4. send once, record the row, store the answer under the send_id. Every answer after step 2 is stored, because the page spends a send_id once the server has answered (amendment 3): a repeat can only be a retry of a request whose answer never arrived. The reservation is deleted only when an unexpected error lands before Twilio is called, so that id may retry; one left with no answer past SEND_STALE_SECONDS answers INBOX_SEND_UNRECORDED, so a crashed request never blocks a draft for good.
│           ├── thread.js     # The opened item's message list (spec 4.2), PURE. Every event with the person in the last 30 days plus anything older still unanswered, oldest first, with system lines for proposal sends, calls, opt-outs, the taps, promises, and a close the thread could not otherwise explain. Times stay ISO; the page renders them in Chicago time. Clock words inside a line are Chicago time.
│           ├── zolaLeads.js  # Zola lead rows (spec 2026-10-14 section 5.6), the WRITER. It runs inside one message's transaction (the sync passes that transaction's client as db), so every query goes through db and nothing here takes a pool connection of its own. Lane zola-leads adds applyNotice, the client creation and the daily cap here; this lane stores every gated notice against a lead row and no more.
│           └── zolaMail.js   # Zola's notices (spec 2026-10-14 section 5.6), PURE. This module is the ONE home of the authentication gate and the subject match; lane zola-leads adds the body parser (parseZola) here and never redefines either.
└── thumbtack-agent/
```

<!-- /gen:tree -->

### What the tree cannot describe

The old hand tree's descriptions that no header in the generated tree says, kept as they were: `docs/` (shown by name alone), the directories of audio and images, the two JSON files and `AGENTS.md`, which take no header comment, and `client/src/index.css`, whose leading comment is about its fonts.

- `docs/`: Project docs. The three the workflow actually runs on:
  - `fix-list-remaining-2026-07-02.md`: THE rolling backlog, the one list (`tech-debt.md`, `open-threads.md` and the staff-ops backlog were folded in and DELETED 2026-08-13)
  - `walkthroughs-owed.md`: the one file tracking owed walkthroughs; never scatter "owed X" fragments
  - `build-board.md`: Claude-maintained ready/in-flight/shipped lane index (write via `scripts/board-write.sh`)

  plus runbooks (`ops-runbook.md`, `va-calling-runbook.md`, `runbooks/override-conversion.md`), project docs (`client-portal-v2-project.md`, `staff-portal-beo-project.md`, `client-portal-design-reference.md`), the per-surface `*-design-prompt.md` set, `design-artifacts/`, `audit-2026-07-13/`, and `superpowers/{specs,plans}/`.
- `server/assets/`: Static bundled audio served to Twilio `<Play>`: `voicemail-greeting.mp3` (Zul, 2026-07-24) + primary-greeting-day/-night, primary-escalate-ack/-failed (Dallas, 2026-08-19; 8kHz mono, the rate a phone line carries).
- `client/src/images/`: Brand assets. `images/marketing/` holds the 8 public-website photos, kept under `src/` (NOT `public/`) on purpose: webpack content-hashes them into `/static/media/`, which Vercel already serves `s-maxage=31536000, immutable`. Anything hand-placed in `public/` ships without a hash and Vercel then serves it `max-age=0, must-revalidate`, so every repeat visitor re-validates it. Reference them with an `import`, never a `/images/...` string.
- `client/src/index.css`: Global styles.
- `client/package.json`: React deps, proxy: localhost:5000. `build` = plain `react-scripts build` and must stay a single command: the sha gate (`scripts/lib/shaGate.js`) spawns `npx react-scripts build` directly in its worktree, so any extra step chained here would never be exercised by the gate that claims to run what Vercel runs.
- `client/vercel.json`: Host-gated redirects (admin/staff/public split) + the SPA rewrite. Deliberately carries NO `headers` cache rule: the SPA catch-all rewrites any missing path to index.html with a 200, so a cache rule on an asset prefix would also cache a typo'd URL's index.html for the full TTL. Content-hashed /static/ assets need no rule.
- `AGENTS.md`: Pointers for an external agent: `docs/money-laws.md`, `docs/dev.md`, `.claude/CLAUDE.md`.

### Descriptions waiting for a header

The old hand tree described these client directories and page groups file by file in one place. The header migration (2026-10-27) gave most of their files a placeholder header generated from the file's exports instead (it ends `replace with what it does`), so these words are the only description those files have. They are kept here as they were; when a file's header is written from them, delete its words here.

client/src/pages/staff/: Staff portal - the live v2 portal, mounted at root on staff.drbartender.com (HomePage, ShiftsPage + ShiftDetail (+ RequestMoreTime, the lazy-loaded request-more-time card ShiftDetail opens mid-event: 30-minute end-time picker, hosted product-confirmation checkbox, request-sent confirmation; no price ever shown to staff), PayPage + PayoutDetail, TipCardPage, EmailVerifyPage email-change confirm) + DownloadTipSign, the standalone page a bartender opens to download their bar sign (DownloadTipSign.jsx + .css) drawing tipCard/ (sizes.js canvas table + the PER-SURFACE capture scale, since the sign is authored at 150 DPI and captured at 2x while the card is native 300 and captured at 1x; PaymentMarks.jsx shared pieces + design-token import; typeFit.js shared canvas name-measurement + the font-ready re-measure hook; SignLayout.jsx the 5x7 bar sign, exactly 5x7 with no bleed because a photo counter prints at a named size and never trims (one size: a QR wants ~1/10 its scan distance plus 20-30% in low light, and a bar is low light); BizCardLayout.jsx the two-sided hand-out card, PORTRAIT 2x3.5in on a 675x1125 bleed artboard because it does go to a press that trims; PhoneSignLayout.jsx the display-mode layout at the device's own 9:19.5 proportions; renderToFile.js the single html2canvas capture feeding JPG/PNG/PDF)
client/src/pages/staff/account/: AccountPage shell + sub-nav with ProfileSection, PaymentMethodsSection (+ PaymentMethodRows + AddMethodModal), CalendarSyncSection, NotificationsSection (+ IOSCoachmark + PushPermissionBanner), DocumentsSection (+ ReplaceConfirmModal)
client/src/pages/plan/: PlannerRouter (planner_version switch) -> legacy PotionPlanningLab (v1 wizard, steps/ components/ data/ intact for in-flight drafts; components/PaymentReturnNotice.js is the post-Stripe return box both v1 and v2 celebrations share: received on a card, processing on a bank debit, spec 2026-09-14) OR v2/ (planner v2: PlannerV2 orchestrator, queue.js, steps/ x11 - no payment UI, hosted three-shape picker, crowd questions); lab/ (Enhancement Lab page at /plan/:token/lab: EnhancementLab orchestrator + LabShelves + LabLedger, invoice-only additions)
client/src/pages/invoice/: InvoicePage - public token-gated invoice view + payment
client/src/pages/proposal/: ProposalView (public client-facing), split into proposalView/ folder (parent + ProposalHeader + ProposalPricingBreakdown + SignAndPaySection + PaymentForm + SwitchBanner post-switch banner with undo + AgreementText markdown-lite renderer + helpers + styles + paidState.js (row truth for the paid card and terms box; readRedirect) + settlePoll.js (bounded post-redirect poll) + useSettle.js (ref-latched settle phase) + checkoutVisibility.js (pure checkout gating: settling / pending / pay controls) + PaidCard.js + PaymentTermsBox.js + intentQuote.js (merges a create-intent quote into the SNAPSHOT only; the projection never touches proposal.total_price) + pageRows.js (the pricing rows: the server's folded price_rows plus the gratuity row from the snapshot) + gratuityFloor.js) + compare/ (ProposalCompare thin wrapper for the option-group page at /compare/:token (and, with `preview`, the admin-only /compare/:token/preview: drafts included, no redirects, choose buttons off) + PackageMatrix aligned live-priced compare grid; its ExplorePackagesSection export is superseded by otherOptions/ and no longer referenced) + otherOptions/ (the options LADDER drawer: OtherOptionsPanel shell + Rung presentation + ladder.js assembly + ExtrasPanel anchor-scoped strip + switchApi.js the one write + popularExtras.js curated chips; pin-to-compare and CompareTable are retired) + catalogBuckets.js (shared package-contents bucketing)
client/src/pages/public/: Client portal (ClientLogin, ClientShoppingList, Blog, BlogPost) + tip flow (TipPage with TipPage.atoms.jsx + TipPage.css, TipPageThanks post-tip feedback, TipSignDisplay full-screen bar-top display mode at /tip/:token/display: public and token-gated so a venue tablet needs no login, fetches ?view=sign which carries no payment handles, renders PhoneSignLayout at the device's own proportions rather than scaling the 5x7 sheet, holds a screen wake lock re-acquired on visibility change)
client/src/pages/public/portal/: Client Portal v2 - PortalHome (landing), EventCommandCenter (focus shell), OverviewWidgets, ArchiveList, ShareButton, EmptyStates, ChangeRequestForm (request-a-change form with live price preview), money/nextUp/constants helpers + tabs/ (OverviewTab, PrescriptionTab, PotionTab, ReceiptsTab, ChangeRequestBanner pending/decided status banner on the Prescription tab)
client/src/pages/website/: Public website (HomePage, ServicesPage, PackagesPage, MethodPage, AboutPage, FaqPage, QuotePage, ClassWizard, quoteWizard/ - split QuoteWizard with steps/extras/ (AddonTile + BundlePicker + AddonAccordion) for the Extras step redesign; legal/ - LegalLayout + PrivacyPage + TermsPage at /privacy + /terms, the URLs submitted for Twilio A2P campaign review; SmsOptInPage at /sms - the standalone SMS opt-in form, also submitted for A2P review, whose path is referenced by the campaign and must not be renamed casually)
```

<!-- /gen:scripts -->

Script notes:

- `npm test`: **These tests share the dev database**, so run them ONE AT A TIME from the repo root, never in parallel and never from a lane worktree while another run is live. Two concurrent runs stomp each other's rows and produce failures that have nothing to do with your change.
- `npm run test:smoke`: Run the money-path smoke by hand (the sha gate runs it inside `npm run ship`): reset the isolated Neon `ci-smoke` branch, apply the schema, and run the money suites serially. No `NEON_API_KEY` → prints a loud SKIP banner and exits 0, which the sha gate reads as never a pass. See [Test gate](#test-gate).
- `npm run check:css-scope`: Assert the legacy Apothecary palette stays out of the Admin OS shell: no unscoped bare-element rule painting an Apothecary token (`inherit`/`currentColor`/a non-palette literal are fine; `@keyframes` steps are out of scope), and no admin-reachable class rule painting an Apothecary `:root` token below 3:1 in a skin that has no skin-aware admin override. Exits non-zero on a finding, so it can gate CI. Also runs on pre-commit whenever `client/src/index.css` is staged, but **warn-only there on purpose** while the parser burns in: it prints and never blocks the commit, because a false positive on the repo's largest file would cost you `--no-verify`, which also skips `check-file-size`, lint-staged and `guard-os-main`.
- `npm run worktree:new -- <name>`: Create a parallel-dev worktree at `../worktrees/<name>` on a new branch off `main`, with `node_modules`, husky, and BOTH env files (`.env` + `client/.env`) symlinked in. `client/.env` matters for running, not just committing: it carries `REACT_APP_API_URL`, and without it CRA bakes an empty API base into the bundle and every call 500s against the dev server.
- `npm run worktree:rm -- <name>`: Tear down a worktree: remove its symlinks, the worktree, then the branch (`--force` to discard an unmerged branch).
- `npm run lane:status`: List open lanes (worktrees) and flag stale ones (48h no-commit, 15+ main commits since cut, or a sensitive path landed on main since cut). The `SessionStart` hook also runs it (`--session-start`, a short form that never fails the session) at every session start; see `docs/dev.md` > Lanes and ships.
- `npm run seed`: Create admin account from `ADMIN_EMAIL`/`ADMIN_PASSWORD`.
- `npm run admin:create`: Promote an existing user to admin (or create one) from `ADMIN_EMAIL`/`ADMIN_PASSWORD`.
- `npm run test:css-scope`: Test the palette-scope checker itself (`scripts/check-css-palette-scope.test.js`): asserts index.css is clean today, and re-arms the trap in memory to prove the checker still goes red. Separate from `npm test` on purpose: that glob is `server/**` and those suites share the dev database, while this one is pure text analysis.
- `npm run mobile:check`: Dev-only phone-viewport (390x844) screenshot + overflow probe of every client-facing surface (`scripts/mobile-capture.js`); merge gate for the mobile-fixes lanes. EXPECTED to stay red on main until the mobile-sweep lane lands: the baseline failures are the audited P0s, not regressions.

## Deployment

### Backend — Render
The production service (`os`) was created in the Render dashboard and is not Blueprint-managed; its real settings and the rollback lever are recorded in `docs/dev.md`. Push to `main` auto-deploys.

### Frontend — Vercel
The React app is deployed separately on Vercel. `client/vercel.json` handles SPA routing. The build-time `REACT_APP_API_URL` points to the Render backend.

### Database
Schema is auto-initialized on server start via `server/db/index.js`, which runs `schema.sql`. All DDL is idempotent (`IF NOT EXISTS`, `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`).

### Test gate

Because a push to `main` **is** the deploy, nothing reaches it ungated. `npm run ship` (and `scripts/merge-lane.sh ship`, which runs the same flow) runs `node scripts/push-gate.js sha <sha>` before it pushes, and `.husky/pre-push` refuses a push whose sha has no passing receipt. The gate tests the commit in a throwaway worktree of its own (`<worktrees dir>/.gate-<sha7>`), picks its checks from everything in `origin/main..<sha>` (never the tip alone), and writes a receipt keyed on the sha; the hook only reads that receipt and the review receipts, so nothing slow runs inside git's SSH window (on 2026-08-14 an eight-minute hook let GitHub close the connection and a push failed after passing everything). The checks:

- **Money smoke** (when the range touches `server/`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, or the gate's own machinery; `scripts/lib/gatePaths.js` holds the list). It resets the isolated Neon `ci-smoke` branch (a prod-parented copy) to its parent, fetches its connection URI, runs `initDb` against it (which also validates any `schema.sql` change *before* prod boot replays it), then runs the money-path suites in `scripts/money-smoke-list.txt` serially. Any failure fails the gate. Slow, rate-limiter-bound suites are deliberately excluded.
- **Client build** (when the range touches `client/`): the exact `CI=true react-scripts build` Vercel runs, catching CI-fatal ESLint warnings.
- **Static checks** (when the range touches `server/` or `client/`): `scripts/static-checks.js`; a blocking finding fails the gate.

A range that needs none of them (docs only, or only files outside `server/`, `client/`, the money paths and the install paths) runs nothing, and its receipt says so, bar the docs check (`gen-docs.js --check --ref <sha>`), which runs for any range with a path outside `docs/`, a `README.md` or `ARCHITECTURE.md` edit included. "Docs-only" means exactly that: a `package-lock.json` bump or an edit to the gate scripts needs the money smoke even with no `server/` change. `docs/dev.md` (The sha gate) has the receipt's fields, the markers and the test overrides.

**Credential: `NEON_API_KEY`.** The money smoke reads the key from `process.env.NEON_API_KEY` or `~/.secrets/neon_api_key` (trimmed). Without it the smoke prints a loud red SKIP banner and the gate records the money check as not passed, so a range that needs it cannot ship: a skip is never a pass. The gate worktree writes its own `.env` of throwaway values with no live key; the box's `.env` never reaches it. To set the key up:

1. `console.neon.tech` → account settings → **API keys** → **Create API key**.
2. Save it: `mkdir -p ~/.secrets && printf '%s' '<key>' > ~/.secrets/neon_api_key && chmod 600 ~/.secrets/neon_api_key` (no trailing newline needed — the runner trims). Never commit it.

The connection URI and the API key are never printed (masked in every error). Run the smoke by hand any time with `npm run test:smoke`.

**Emergency escape:** `git push --no-verify` (or `HUSKY=0`) skips the hook's check: deliberate, per-push and visible.

## Operational Runbook

### Weekly dispute-email-bailout sweep

The dispute-won email notification (fires on Stripe `charge.dispute.funds_reinstated`) auto-abandons after 3 failed send attempts. The DB column `tips.dispute_email_failed_at` is the canonical "needs manual reconciliation" marker; the accompanying Sentry alert is best-effort.

**Weekly:** run the sweep query documented in `ARCHITECTURE.md` ("Weekly dispute-email-bailout sweep") to catch any abandonment whose Sentry alert was lost. The spec at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-25-dispute-email-retry-bailout-design.md` carries the recovery runbook.
